❯ Welcome back! It is now 11/15/2026 and we're ready to start us a new dev day.
  The prior dev day chat record still needs closing out and summarizing before
  dev begins, and I'd also ask that you include in your new dev day bootstrap
  anything which should be brought to my attention which I clearly missed in
  the prior chat despite it being asked for a response from me. These modern
  code harnesses can sometimes make it tricky to scroll to the proper place to
  locate all the "your eyes needed on this" sections in a long chat. Not the
  end of the world if we try to recollect and bring back those items so I can
  take a look again.

  Based on the items I did see in need of my attention, I've noticed that the
  directory membranes in the 2D Local Map still aren't exceptionally _useful_
  (interaction-wise) at high zoom-outs because there are no buttons to interact
  with at that distance.

  In addition, I saw that the branch-renderer.ts node you linked me to contains
  a tremendous number of backwards-reaching connector wires. In spite of this,
  the 3D button does not glow to indicate that the 2D local map is hitting its
  upper limit in what it can draw strictly along a left-to-right dependency
  direction.

  Stylistically, there remain more little nits than we can count -- things that
  a formal designer would know the terminology to call out immediately (like
  "font hierarchy") but I presently lack (and still feel like I'm not really
  being taught all that much? I remember the root agent was also supposed to be
  the pedagogical bridge between what Live Documentation could teach me and
  what the system actually saliently contained/was-composed-of? I feel like
  that effort has possibly fallen by the wayside?)

  Fable 5.2 (you) has solid training data which has substantially improved
  human-like vision capabilities that were previously restricted to just GPT-6
  Astra. Put another way: you currently have a tad bit more of a designer's eye
  than I now do. So long as your preferences aren't in tremendous direct
  conflict with my own and aren't something we've already tried and thrown
  away, I want you to exercise a little more design freedom to ensure that the
  layout results are maximally understandable and aesthetically pleasing.

  Let's start.

      23 +- **A freer hand in design, inside their preferences and the re
         +cord** (2026-11-15, [Turn 1](../ChatHistory/2026/11/2026-11-15.
         +1.record.md#turn-1)). "Fable 5.2 (you) has solid training data 
         +which has substantially improved human-like vision capabilities
         + that were previously restricted to just GPT-6 Astra. Put anoth
         +er way: you currently have a tad bit more of a designer's eye t
         +han I now do. So long as your preferences aren't in tremendous 
         +direct conflict with my own and aren't something we've already 
         +tried and thrown away, I want you to exercise a little more des
         +ign freedom to ensure that the layout results are maximally und
         +erstandable and aesthetically pleasing." Said after "more littl
         +e nits than we can count" in the 2D map's style. **How to apply
         +** (the root's reading): the two conditions are the owner's and
         + both are checked against the record before a design is built, 
         +their standing preferences and what was tried and rejected; ins
         +ide them the root designs as a designer would, builds it as wha
         +t the preview shows, says what changed and why, and no longer h
         +olds an ordinary visual call for their yes. A change to what th
         +e product means or does is still a fork for them, and in a disa
         +greement about a picture their eye still decides.              
      24 +                                                               
      25 +- **Teach the designer's words as the work goes; the root is th
         +e bridge, and that had lapsed** (2026-11-15, same turn). Of the
         + style nits, "things that a formal designer would know the term
         +inology to call out immediately (like "font hierarchy") but I p
         +resently lack (and still feel like I'm not really being taught 
         +all that much? I remember the root agent was also supposed to b
         +e the pedagogical bridge between what Live Documentation could 
         +teach me and what the system actually saliently contained/was-c
         +omposed-of? I feel like that effort has possibly fallen by the 
         +wayside?)". They remember rightly: "the Root Agent is also the 
         +pedagogical bridge for the user -- between what we wish our sof
         +tware could do and what it really does" is their sentence of 20
         +26-11-06 ([Turn 26](../ChatHistory/2026/11/2026-11-05.1.record.
         +md#turn-26--november-6)), it went into AGENTS.md that day (`e10
         +4f9fa`), and the cut of 2026-11-12 took the phrase out (`06f94c
         +84`) and the day's restoration did not bring it back; it is res
         +tored on 2026-11-15. It joins "Teach the concepts at the decisi
         +on point" (2026-11-03) below. **How to apply:** a reply that la
         +nds or proposes visual work names the principle it rests on in 
         +the designer's own term, says in a sentence what the term means
         +, and points at where it shows on our own screen, before and af
         +ter; an accepted round is walked through the Explorer with the 
         +code that carries it pinned, saying what the product could not 
         +yet show. Not a lecture and not a glossary dumped at the end: t
         +he term arrives with the decision it explains.

  1. The 3D button on branch-renderer.ts: a defect, being fixed. The 2D map puts
  what a file uses to its left. When files use each other in a ring, one
  connection has to run against that order, and the map draws it as a small loop
  that shows its full route on hover. That route is the wire in your
  screenshots (AI-Agent-Workspace/Screenshots/2026-11-15/designer-eye/02-returni
  ng-connection-on-hover.png). The cue built on 11/2 only counts a forward
  connection whose path happens to double back, and skips these returning ones.
  On that file the page draws eight returning routes and counts zero. Your words
  on 11/2 described this exact wire, with "Next case I find I'll point out".
  The builder is making the count include them and giving the cue a real glow in
  two strengths, with no text.

  directory-three-presses-out.png) and after Fit (AI-Agent-Workspace/Screenshots
  /2026-11-15/designer-eye/04-opened-directory-after-fit.png):

  - "Open" is a text button that shrinks to 7 px and vanishes on narrow boxes;
    five of seven closed directories have nothing to press.
  - An opened directory loses its close control entirely.
  - Its name shrinks to about 6 px beside file names held at 13, and is cut to
    "…plorer/src/client" with 900 px of free room beside it.

  What I'm having built:

  - A closed directory opens when you click anywhere on its box.
  - A chevron (the small arrow a file tree uses) replaces both the "Open" text
    and the close cross.
  - An opened directory's name is a handle that closes it.
  - A directory's name is never smaller than the file names inside it, and its
    parent path is cut before the name is.

  3. Style. I looked at sixteen pictures of the current build and wrote up what
  a designer would call out, each term next to the place it shows: the reading,
  with ten pictures
  (AI-Agent-Workspace/Screenshots/2026-11-15/designer-eye/README.md). The three
  that matter most:

  - Hierarchy is the order your eye meets things, set by size, weight and
    brightness. On a card today (AI-Agent-Workspace/Screenshots/2026-11-15/desig
    ner-eye/01-reading-card-one-tone.png) every text is the same grey and a
    symbol's description is larger than its name, so you read prose before
    names. The fix is brightness alone, since yesterday's work fixed the sizes:
    names bright, prose a step down (AI-Agent-Workspace/Screenshots/2026-11-15/d
    esigner-eye/08-probe-tone-reading-card.png).
  - Figure and ground is what stands out against what recedes. Every wire glows
    at rest, so on localView (AI-Agent-Workspace/Screenshots/2026-11-15/designer
    -eye/06-crowded-directory-as-built.png) the lines outshine the names.
    Without the resting glow (AI-Agent-Workspace/Screenshots/2026-11-15/designer
    -eye/09-probe-tone-crowded-directory.png), names lead and a hovered card's
    lines still light up.
  - Greeking is drawing text too small to read as grey bars, so a page keeps its
    shape. At full zoom-out a card is a name over a tall blank box (AI-Agent-Wo
    rkspace/Screenshots/2026-11-15/designer-eye/05-overview-blank-cards.png).
    With each row drawn as a faint bar (AI-Agent-Workspace/Screenshots/2026-11-1
    5/designer-eye/10-probe-rows-as-bars.png), a tall card reads as a file with
    many symbols, and nothing moves. I think this beats the files-only trial,

  ## Forks I decided
  - **Symbol name's colour is stated twice.** `local.css:243` still says `color:
  var(--text-secondary)` for `.local-column .symbol-label`, a line the brief
  put outside what I may touch. I override it from `reading.css` (later in the
  import order, same specificity; the hover rule still wins). The clean form is
  a one-token change on `local.css:243` and deleting my `reading.css` line. Say
  so and it is done.
  - **Lit laces glow too.** The filled polygons at a pin never had the glow; the
  single lit rule now gives it to every lit `.connection-path`.
  - **Two existing expectations changed** to follow your decision that a lit
  wire is at full strength: Flow's hovered-card lines were asserted "same
  opacity as rest", and the bundle run was a literal 0.45. The Flow rule's
  comment and the design paragraph say "lit" where they said "keep their look".
  - **Legend geometry.** Wire samples are 20 × 2 px (were 3 px; a wire is 2.2),
  dots 12 px as a row's dot (were 10), and the test-backed sample is a 20 × 14
  halo with a card inset 2 px, both in the card's own variables.

  arrival and three presses out, so I took no second-value pictures.
  - **Pictures use a fresh visitor's real defaults**, continuing search on and
  waited for, not the suite's `searchStarts: 0`.

  - **A path's wires cannot be lit in 2D.** They carry no class of their own

  now.

  builder's) uses `var(--border)` and its comment says "at the membrane's own

● The tone round's first handback is reviewed and sent back once; the other two
  builders are still working. One correction to what I told you earlier:

  Lit wires will not get brighter than resting ones. My brief to the builder had
  a hovered wire drawn stronger than a wire at rest. That breaks your oldest
  rule for this map: fade the irrelevant, never boost the relevant. I caught it
  on review, compared three strengths side by side, and found the glow was the
  whole problem. So the wires keep their old strength, the glow goes entirely,
  and a hover still only fades the others. Two test expectations the builder had
  changed to fit my mistake go back to what they were.

  What the tone builder got right and found:

  - Names now lead on a card. File name and symbol names bright, descriptions a
    step down. Contrast is 14 and 13 to 1 for names and 7 to 1 for prose, where
    everything was 9.8.
  - The legend was wrong in three places. The "Test-backed" sample was a gold
    glow, but the card it describes has a purple border. The wire samples were a
    different blue and green from the real wires, and the dot samples were
    smaller than real dots. All three now match what the picture draws.

  I also asked it to measure what removing the glow does to the 2D map's speed
  with a lot pinned, since those filters were already known to be the most
  expensive part of a moving frame.

  Nothing is merged yet and nothing is pushed (three commits today, all records
  and evidence). I'll send links on 8999 as each round lands.

  The 3D button now lights for a cycle's returning connections. On
  `branch-renderer.ts` opened alone it lights with 8 at the stronger strength: 8
  is the number of `.back-route` paths drawn, and the cards' feet add up to 16,
  since each return is told at both of its ends. The number stays 8 at the
  reading, connections and overview looks and with files only. In 3D the cue is
  off.

  Branch `worktree-agent-a843e390ed894c27f`, built on `cb53d7c7`. Nothing is
  pushed.

  a stray wire
  - `4c0a3cd4` Set the 3D cue's stronger strength from how often pictures light
  it, and document the cue

  ### What changed
  - **`packages/explorer/src/client/views/localView/returning-connections.ts`**
  (new, pure) and its `.test.ts`:
    - `returningConnections(links, isBack, drawn)` returns `{ edges, total,
  byFile }`. It skips a file's references to itself and counts a pair line by
  its links.
    - `connectionCount`, `backwardStrength`, `BACKWARD_STRENGTHS` and
  `MANY_BACKWARD = 5`.
  - **`connections.ts`**:
    - `drawConnections` now counts the returning references (once each) plus the
  forward wires that double back. The latter are renamed `doubling` in both
  bands.
    - `drawsReference(getAnchor, edge)` (exported) is true when neither of the
  reference's rows is `.branch-symbol-hidden`.
    - A reference whose end is still fading does not count. That check is
  `presenceOf`, now shared with the anchor measurer.
  - **`branch-renderer.ts` `dressCards`**:
    - It first discloses or hides every card's rows, then makes the foot and its
  menu from the same `returningConnections` + `drawsReference` the button uses.
    - The foot is per card and counts returns at either end; the button counts
  each return once for the picture.
  - **`controller.ts`**: a move's frames no longer report 0, and the final frame
  reports. Before, the cue went out and lit again at every move, which would
  also have pulsed it every time.
  - **`index.ts` cue block**:
    - Adds `data-backward="some|many"` beside the `has-backward-wires` class.
    - Adds a `backward-rising` class only when the strength rises, removed at
  `animationend`.
    - The name stays "3D". The tooltip is the accessible description; this is
  how the toolbar's text buttons are named.
  - **`styles/local.css`**: I touched only the cue's rules, about line 321; that
  one rule became a short block.
    - Amber border, amber label and a soft glow.
    - Many: a deeper glow and an amber-tinted face.
    - One 1.8 s swell, with no animation under `prefers-reduced-motion`.
  - **`tests/e2e/backward-cue.spec.ts`** (new, 6 cases). The existing cases in
  `local-map-branches.spec.ts` are untouched and all 11 pass. That includes line
  314 (the five-file picture keeps title "Local Map in 3D"); it draws no
  returns.
  - **Docs**:
    - A paragraph dated November 15 in
  `.mdmd/layer-3/live-documentation-explorer.mdmd.md`, after the 2D/3D controls
  sentence in "Independent exploration", with the measurement.
    - Authored notes appended to the Live Docs of `connections.ts`,
  `branch-renderer.ts`, `controller.ts`, `index.ts` and `local.css`, plus the
  three new Live Docs.
    - All cite `2026-11-15.1.record.md#turn-1`.

  ## What removing the glow buys
  Measured on `/?view=local&node=packages/explorer/src/client/types.ts`: 373
  wires drawn, 263 glowing before, 0 after.

  Method: the November 6 probe's (`zoom-perf.cjs` and `zoom-perf-wheel.cjs`):
  CDP performance metrics and animation-frame intervals over 1.2 s after each of
  three moves, default tuning, search off, headless 1440 × 960. Those scripts
  require `node_modules` under the working directory, which a worktree lacks, so
  I ran the same method from a scratch copy with ordinary imports and one
  variant; the probe itself is untouched.

  Two passes with the builds in opposite order. One-minute load was 6.3 then 3.9
  across pass 1, and 5.1 then 9.2 across pass 2. Each cell is pass 1, then pass
  2.

  | Move | Build | Frames in 1.2 s | Longest frame (ms) | Main-thread task time
  (s) |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | Wheel out, 1st | before | 36, 41 | 450, 550 | 2.56, 3.81 |
  | | after | 74, 54 | 17, 67 | 0.43, 0.66 |
  | Wheel out, 2nd | before | 12, 9 | 617, 1217 | 6.85, 9.00 |
  | | after | 16, 12 | 333, 500 | 0.77, 1.18 |
  | Wheel out, 3rd | before | 20, 21 | 967, 1150 | 6.03, 7.39 |
  | | after | 67, 47 | 83, 117 | 0.77, 1.09 |
  | Press, 1st | before | 54, 51 | 233, 250 | 0.46, 0.50 |
  | | after | 69, 66 | 100, 133 | 0.24, 0.25 |
  | Press, 2nd | before | 72, 70 | 33, 50 | 0.11, 0.15 |
  | | after | 73, 71 | 33, 33 | 0.12, 0.15 |
  | Press, 3rd (change to connections) | before | 14, 11 | 483, 867 | 1.19, 1.70
  |
  | | after | 32, 12 | 433, 800 | 0.84, 1.54 |

  - **Wheel zoom**: main-thread task time falls to a sixth to a ninth, and the
  longest frame from 0.45–1.2 s to 17–120 ms, except the second wheel zoom on
  the new build (333 and 500 ms).
  - **Press**: a press that changes no look gains only on its first frame. The
  press that changes the look is dominated by restyling every card, and the glow
  was a small part of it.
  - **Caveat**: the two builds report the change to the connections look one
  wheel move apart under the same input (before by the end of the 2nd, after by
  the end of the 3rd). I did not chase that, so the 2nd and 3rd wheel rows
  compare slightly different moves.
  - **Caveat**: this is a software renderer on a loaded machine; the ratios are
  the finding, not the milliseconds.

  The two passes' output is kept as `frame-cost-pass1.json` and
  `frame-cost-pass2.json`.

  ## What changed, and where
  - **New pure module**
  `packages/explorer/src/client/views/localView/directory-handle.ts` (+
  `.test.ts`, 9 cases): the strip's constant (`HANDLE_STRIP` = 60), the chevron,
  `handleParts` (parent path / own name), `handleRun` (how far across the strip
  the handle may reach), `fitHandle` (a closed box's name brought down in size
  before it breaks inside a word).
  - **Closed box** (`card-factory.ts`, `card-reading.ts`, `reading.css`,
  `local.css`): a click anywhere on the box opens it, as do Enter and Space. The
  name is the handle: a button led by a chevron pointing right, labelled `Open
  <path>`, `aria-expanded="false"`, with a tooltip. The "Open" text button is
  gone. Inspect is in More (it was already there as "Inspect directory"). In the
  far looks the chevron is held with the name and the name is fitted.
  - **Opened or pinned-membrane label** (`branch-renderer.ts`, `local.css`): one
  line — chevron (down when opened, right when only encasing), parent path
  muted, own name in the primary tone at weight 600, the "+N files" count, More,
  then the ancestry control. A click on the chevron or the name closes or
  opens; the close cross is gone. Only the line's parts take the pointer, so a
  click on the empty strip does nothing and still pans.
  - **Size**: from the middle band out the whole line is held at `--text-scale`,
  capped at 3.6, by CSS alone. `roomsOfLabel` and the separate held drawing are
  deleted.
  - **Room**: the label is a strip of constant height, so the layout no longer
  measures label text. In Flow, `branch-placement.ts` now keeps the label's room
  above the first member of every column (it was the leftmost only), and the
  label's width is set at each pose to the run of leading columns whose tops are
  level. Outside Flow it keeps the leftmost column, as before.
  - **Same number elsewhere**: `directory-flow.ts` `NOMINAL_LABEL` and the
  layout lab's `labelHeight` (`scripts/layout-lab/card-model.ts`, `measurer.ts`)
  now read `HANDLE_STRIP`.
  - **Keyboard focus** (`controller.ts`, `branch-renderer.ts`): after opening
  from the keyboard the focus now lands on the opened handle, and after closing
  on the box's handle. On `cb53d7c7` it stayed on the fading box and dropped to
  `body`; I traced both builds.
  - **Dead code removed**: `inspectDirectory` in the controller and
  `CardContext`, and the `onInspectDirectory` option — one line in
  `client/index.ts`, not the perspective controls.
  - **Specs touched**: `local-map-directories` (new case), `zoom-looks`
  (closed-box case rewritten), `card-ladder-boxes`, `closed-directory-looks`,
  `labels-and-controls`, `inspection`, `local-map-framing`, `still-picture.ts`.

  ## Forks I decided
  1. A one-line label in a constant strip, rather than a wrapping label.
  2. The pressable handle is the chevron and the name, not the whole strip, so
  the strip cannot close a directory by accident.
  3. The strip runs across columns in Flow only; outside Flow that would be a
  wider layout change.
  4. A closed box's held name may use the box down to 2 units above its foot (66
  units, two lines at 2.2).
  5. In the closed box at reading size the count is indented under the name, and
  More keeps its own row so no box changes size.
  6. The parent path's cut is left out of the audit's cut-text list
  (`still-picture.ts`), because it is the design; the own name and the count are
  audited.
  7. More may stand on the line under a fitted name in a narrow box (`scripts`
  three presses out), as on a file card.

  ## Forks handed back
  - **A. Nested open directory (the fault).** On `packages/engine/src` with
  `live-docs` opened, the row `config / languages / tooling` stands 79 units
  lower than the strip needs, 139 below the handle. Cause: "from the top" pulls
  every item to one floating ceiling, which levels the first item of every
  column, and the columns where `live-docs` rises to the top now begin under two
  strips. Options:
    - (i) keep it;
    - (ii) return to a strip in the leftmost column only — the handle is then
  one column wide and `adapters` stands 60 lower than its neighbours (30 today);
    - (iii) give a membrane its strip only in its level run — about 19 units of
  gap would remain by my arithmetic;
    - (iv) change what "from the top" levels.

    I would take (iii) or (iv); both are layout decisions beyond "the strip's
  height".
  - **B. More in the closed box at reading size** sits alone on a row under the
  count. I would move it to the name's line at the right, as on a file card,
  together with milestone 2's rework of the cells' field.
  - **C. At reading size** there are 45 empty units between the handle and the
  first row.
  - **D. Handles off screen.** With a file as subject the camera stands inside
  large membranes, and their handles are off screen at three, five and seven
  presses out, as before. A handle that stays in view was not in the brief and
  is not built.

  - What it counts: every connection the picture draws against left-to-right.
    The old cue missed a cycle's returning connections, which is nearly all of
    them.
  - Signal versus noise is the designer's concern here: a cue that is always on
    stops meaning anything. The builder opened 161 files one at a time; 89% draw
    no backward connection, 9% draw one to four, 2% draw five or more (yours is
    one). So it has two strengths, and amber is used for nothing else on the
    map.

    the returns actually drawn. Ones behind "+N symbols" join the count when
    shown.

  Your answers

  Files only at full zoom-out. It is a configured value, not a reading of your
  screen. The far look starts between the sixth and seventh Zoom Out press,
  where the map is about 30% of reading size, chosen by eye on 11/6. Designers
  call that a breakpoint: a fixed threshold where the layout switches. I'd keep
  it fixed, because the same press then always gives the same look. No pushback
  on your Flow remark either. My alternative (rows drawn as faint bars, nothing
  moves) is nearly built; you'll get both side by side, pick one, and I'll
  delete the other.

  Cells. It becomes the default. The mark you couldn't read is folders in
  folders: a sub-directory drawn as a tiny skin with micro-dots. It fails
  because it is drawn at a second, smaller size, and size means nothing there.
  I'll have a nested directory drawn as a small skin around same-sized cells.
  For "more directory-shaped" you'll get three matched shapes to choose from:
  today's round skin, a squarer one, and a folder tab with the name on it.

  Kept description line. I withdraw my call to drop it. Your diagnosis is right:
  a row has room for two lines at that zoom, and a long name takes both. You'll
  get alternatives to look at: the name held to one line and cut at its end, or
  cut in its middle so the distinguishing ending survives
  ("Documentation…Result"), each with a description line on every row, plus the
  file's own line under its name.

  Changed Files. The switch is in Tuning → History → Changed Files, on the same
  step (http://127.0.0.1:8999/?s=N4IgbiBcDMA0IEcomgExPAnsgzgFwCcBXAYzyIIFMMQALZS
  gTkdQFZGAmVRgQwDMAHABYA7ACMAjD1QA2PhKGMRPRtDGtWlPqhHRoABgHRGIAL5A). Dim
  already does what you describe: changed files keep their colour and everything
  else fades to a fifth. My link showed you only Glow because a link doesn't
  carry that setting.

  Still pointer. Your reading is right, and your rule is simpler than the three
  modes, so I'll build it and retire the trial. Two details I'm adding:
  - Turning and zooming stay live while the map builds.
  - A pointer that hasn't moved starts nothing when building ends, or the
    surprise just arrives a second later.

  I'll measure how long "building" lasts on big pictures first, and bring the
  number back to you if it is long rather than freeze the map.

  Node 26. Trying it needs no restart and touches no running agent: Node 26 gets
  installed beside 22 inside the container and the full gate runs under it.

  between sessions. One thing to know first: this harness keeps its chat files
  inside the container (/home/node/.claude), so a rebuild discards them. I'll

❯ >Designers call that a breakpoint: a fixed threshold where the layout
  switches.

  Yep, I know about breakpoints. I had instructed that the semantic zoom system
  in the 2D local map be breakpoint-driven after the first iteration that I
  had suggested attempt to keep text size totally constant was a perf
  nightmare. But I don't want to wholly rule out the possibility that the
  "breakpoint" may be a concept we may wish to expand to include "style
  triggerable by _something_", so that we can continue in the vein of
  maximizing competing concerns to produce a good layout _emergently_. That is
  what I believe is genuinely unique and interesting about our layout strategy
  throughtout the software, and I don't want to lose sight of it overall.
  Indeed, perhaps the amber indicator that 2D isn't cutting it anymore could
  also be an indicator to _us_ that we could tweak the layout preferences to
  avoid backwards-facing connections more strongly. We have many levers to
  permute in the 2D and 3D local maps.

  connections possible. · summary

● You're right, and I should have known: breakpoints here were your instruction
  on 11/5 and your "very few breakpoints" hypothesis on 11/8 is what the three
  looks were built from. I explained your own term back to you. I'll check the
  record for your vocabulary before teaching a word.

  Your idea about the amber cue holds up, with numbers. I asked whether the 2D
  layout already reads backward as few connections as any left-to-right order
  could. It doesn't.

  - What I measured: every one of this repository's 968 files opened alone,
    using the layout's own functions (no browser), against the true minimum for
    each picture.
  - Result: 114 pictures read something backward. In 31 of them the layout
    leaves more backward connections than necessary: 425 in total where 357
    would do, about a sixth fewer.
  - Your file: branch-renderer.ts reads 10 backward where 7 is the minimum. (The
    page shows 8 because two are behind "+N symbols".)
  - The limit: no picture goes fully dark. A real cycle always needs at least
    one return.

  Why it happens. When files form a cycle, the layout must pick which
  connections read backward. Today's rule counts files, not connections, so with
  A and B using each other it can send the heavier direction backward. Graph
  drawing calls this choice the minimum feedback arc set. It is hard in general
  but our cycles are tiny (two to four files in 105 of the 114 pictures), so the
  exact answer is cheap.

  What I'm doing: a builder round will make the layout read backward the fewest
  connections, exactly, and keep today's order wherever it is already minimal so
  no picture moves without a gain. It starts when one of the three running
  builders is back.

  On triggers that respond to something. Of the 2D map's four layout steps,
  three already work the emergent way you describe: column assignment, ordering
  within columns, and placement are each found by weighing costs. The one that
  was a fixed rule is this cycle-breaking step, which is why your instinct
  landed on it. The zoom looks are the other fixed thing. The natural candidate
  there is the far view switching when a picture's names stop fitting rather
  than at a fixed press. The cost is that the same press would then give
  different looks on different pictures. I'd hold that until you've chosen
  between files-only and bars.

  ## Forks I decided
  - **No name-length property in `card-factory.ts`.** The card reading already
  writes each row's measured name width (`--row-name-w`), which is truer than a
  character count and keeps me out of the other builder's file.
  - **The name comes first; a covered bar is omitted, not moved.** Bars stay at
  their rows' own places, where names reappear on zooming in. The cost is that a
  card with a long name and one or two rows is still a name over an empty box.
  - **Bars appear at once at the change of look**, as silent rows already leave
  at once; no fade was added.
  - **The fitted name also applies to Flow's compact member cards** in an opened
  directory, since they are slot cards drawn by the same rule. That is the only
  thing "bars" changes on `client`.

› Message from general-purpose
  ## Handback: building-map, first milestone. I stopped at the measurement, as
  the brief says to.

  **Nothing was built.** The brief's rule was to stop if "busy" lasts more than
  about three seconds on the common actions, and it lasts far longer. No product
  code, test, stylesheet or doc changed. Item 5 (making Dim the default for
  Changed Files) is a one-line change that doesn't depend on this, and it isn't
  done either.

  **Commit on branch `worktree-agent-a93801741430a667b`:** `9d1b9a24` "Measure
  how long the 3D Local Map stays busy after the common actions". It adds only
  `AI-Agent-Workspace/Screenshots/2026-11-15/building-map/README.md` (method,
  table, candidates) and `busy-measurements.json` (all 30 raw runs). Base
  `7038efd6`, never amended, not pushed.

  ### How it was measured
  - Headless Chromium on this machine's software renderer, 1440×900, Explorer
  defaults (one hop, Skin, Reveal Frames 60), the 2D continuing search off as in
  the suite.
  - Three fresh-page runs of each action, capped at 60 s.
  - "Busy" is the existing `aria-busy`, timed from the action (for an arrival,
  from page load) until it turns false.
  - I also recorded:
    - the animation frames in that span;
    - the frame by which every sphere had entered;
    - the last frame in which any sphere moved at least 1 px on screen since the
  frame before (camera motion counts).
  - "≈60 fps" is frames ÷ 60, assuming one physics step per frame. It is an
  estimate, not a measurement.

  | Action | Busy, s here (median / worst) | Frames | ≈ s at 60 fps | All
  entered (frame) | Last visible move (frame) |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Estate: arrival on GatewayClient.cs by link | 6.9 / 7.4 | 201 | 3.4 | 13 |
  136 |
  | Estate: click a seed of the archipelago (PaymentService.cs) | 38.2 / 40.7 |
  1,309 | 21.8 | 1 | 186 |
  | Estate: Pin from a sphere's actions | 5.3 / 6.0 | 225 | 3.8 | 6 | 145 |
  | Estate: open a directory (Structure, click the Portal bubble) | 12.6 / 14.1
  | 479 | 8.0 | 1 | 132 |
  | Estate: 2D→3D with a file selected | 7.1 / 7.1 | 298 | 5.0 | 110 | 161 |
  | History sample (7 files): one step | 1.4 / 1.4 | 82 | 1.4 | 3 | none |
  | Repository: arrival on forceGraphView.ts at one hop (133 spheres) | 30.7 /
  32.0 | 207 | 3.5 | 50 | 207 |
  | Repository: click a sphere of that hop (154 spheres) | over 60 (all three
  capped) | over 370 | over 6.2 | 8 | 264 |
  | Repository: 2D→3D on that file | 23.2 / 23.4 | 275 | 4.6 | 118 | 210 |
  | Repository whole in Structure: arrival (1,256 spheres) | over 60 (all
  capped) | over 215 | over 3.6 | 65 | still moving at the cap |

  What the numbers say:
  - **Busy is long everywhere except the tiny sample**, here and in the 60 fps
  estimate. The estate seed click, which is the original fault's journey, is
  about 38 s here and about 22 s at 60 fps.
  - **Busy lasts well past the last visible motion.** On the seed click the
  picture looks still for six sevenths of the busy time (last 1 px move at frame
  186, busy until 1,309). On opening a directory it looks still for nearly
  three quarters. The tail of the motion's stillness test looks worth its own
  investigation, separate from this round.
  - **Every sphere is in early**: by frame 1–50 after a click, pin or arrival,
  and by frame about 110 after a perspective change, whose own animation runs
  first.
  - **Not measured: a history step on this repository.** My worktree's bundle
  has no history (`History: none`), so the 7-file history sample stands in for
  it.
  - **Arrivals are timed from page load**, so those rows overstate busy by the
  load time.

  ### Two definitions of "building", for you to choose
  - **A. Placed and framed (my recommendation).** Building lasts while a body is
  packing, the reveal is still bringing spheres in, or the camera's 650 ms
  framing flight runs. From the table that is about 0.7–2.6 s at 60 fps on every
  action measured. The picture keeps drifting for a few seconds afterwards.
    - Why A: rule 3 (a pointer that hasn't moved since building began starts
  nothing until its first move) is what removes the fault of clicking a seed and
  keeping still. So a short build period is enough, and the map never refuses a
  click for long.
  - **B. Cooled.** This is today's `aria-busy`, the busy column above.
  - **A middle option, also measured: visibly still.** Building lasts until no
  sphere moves 1 px a frame on screen, about 2.2–4.4 s at 60 fps where the
  picture came to rest within the cap. The motion computes nothing on screen
  today, so this needs a new measure inside it.

  ### Decisions for you
  1. **Which definition**, A or B (or the middle).
  2. **If A, what `aria-busy` means.** 26 spec files (74 references) wait for
  `aria-busy="false"` to mean "the picture came to rest".
     - My recommendation: keep `aria-busy` meaning the settle. A new state, for
  example `data-building`, would drive the click and hover block, the `cursor:
  progress` and the sign.
     - The other way, `aria-busy` follows building and the specs move to a new
  rest signal. That touches many files outside my set.
  3. **Blocking sphere drag needs one line in `forceGraphRenderer.ts`, which
  isn't in my file list.** That file grabs a sphere for dragging on its own
  pointerdown. Without that line, "a press on a sphere during building does
  nothing, and it turns the camera instead" can't be done. Approve or refuse.

  ### Groundwork for the build milestone
  - Names and the actions strip can take `pointer-events: none` while building,
  so a press on them during building turns the camera.
  - Keyboard activation (a click with `detail === 0`) stays live.
  - After building ends, the name and strip `pointerenter` handlers must be
  gated as well. The trial found that Chromium sends `pointerenter`, and no
  `pointermove`, to an element that moves under a still pointer.
  - The sign can't go on the toolbar: its rules are in `styles/local.css`, which
  is off limits. A thin line along the top edge of `#graph-svg` in `graph.css`
  fits. This is not yet judged by eye.

  ### Housekeeping
  - **The shared browser lock cost about 10 minutes of waiting.** About 4
  minutes went on a first try that recorded nothing, because of my own bug: the
  tsx runner wraps the page script's function names in a helper the page doesn't
  have. About 5 minutes went on a check I cancelled. The full set of runs then
  waited 38 s and held the lock from 22:52 to 23:08.
  - **No pictures or recording, since nothing was built.** Links to retrace the
  journeys:
    - `/samples/estate/?s=N4IgbiBcDMA0IEcomgExAXyA` (estate archipelago, 3D)
    - `/?s=N4IgbiBcDMA0IEcomgExPAdsgDgQwGMBrPAcwFMBnAenIA8cAbAewCdzXrLWDqDGAluUw
  AXamCEB3GgDM2BcgHFWeHAAsAalIB0IyiAC+QA` (this repository, forceGraphView.ts
  selected, 3D)
  - **Server stopped** by its process id (97721).
  - **No subagent used.**
  - **Kept for the next milestone**, git-ignored and to be deleted when the

● Agent "3D map: no hover while building" finished · 37m 34s

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● One thing you should know about the 3D rule you chose, because the measurement
  changed what "building" can mean.

  The map's internal "busy" state lasts far longer than the map looks busy.
  After a click on a seed in the estate sample it stays busy about 38 seconds on
  this machine (about 22 at full speed, by the builder's estimate). Nothing
  visibly moves after the first sixth of that. Blocking clicks for all of it
  would be worse than the fault.

  So "building" will mean the part you can see: spheres still arriving and the
  camera still flying to frame them, about one to three seconds. During that,
  nodes take no hover or click and the sign shows. Afterwards the map is live
  again, with one rule that removes the original fault: a pointer that hasn't
  moved since the build began starts nothing until you move it. Turning and
  zooming stay live throughout.

  The builder is building that now. It also found something worth its own look
  later: the motion keeps computing for a long time after the picture has
  stopped moving. Our 3D tests wait on that tail, so shortening it could make
  them much faster.

  The 3D map is built as decided, and its new browser spec passes on the build.
  On the base its three cases fail for the right reasons. Dim is now the default
  for Changed Files. The two 3D specs I re-ran (`force-graph-focus` and

  focus spec. The whole browser suite was not run, and one physics unit test

  bugs of mine forced a second hold.

  ### Commits on `worktree-agent-a93801741430a667b`
  All since `7038efd6`; none amended, none pushed.
  - `9d1b9a24`: the measurements from the first milestone.
  - `88f20d26`: while the map builds, its nodes take no hover or click, and the
  map says so.
  - `8b90855b`: Dim becomes the default Changed Files mode.
  - `228f3679`: the design paragraph and the Live Docs.
  - `62d2670e`: fix — the page's first pointer move now counts as a move. The
  spec now checks the refusals before it checks that the map was building.
  - `c6992b0f`: fix — the camera's framing flight counts only for 650 ms from
  its start.
  - `937f48a2`: the evidence (building durations, stills, recordings).
  - `3f4933d4`: `force-graph-focus.spec.ts` now waits for the build to end
  before pressing a sphere.

  ### What changed, by file
  - **`views/forceGraphBuilding.ts` (new) and its unit test.** Three pure
  functions:
    - `nextBuilding`: building starts with "work" (packing, the reveal, a
  perspective change, a change of what is shown). The camera flight continues a
  building but never starts one, so a Fit isn't building. Building ends only
  after the work has been quiet for 200 ms, so one action stays one continuous
  building.
    - `buildingPhase`: `begun` at once, `shown` after 300 ms.
    - `nodePointer`: the permission. Hover is allowed only when the map is not
  building and the pointer has moved since building began. A press is allowed
  whenever the map is not building.
  - **`views/forceGraphView.ts`.**
    - `updateBuilding` runs every frame. It is also called at a membership
  change, a packing wait, the first picture, and `revealForTransition`.
  `transitioning` stays on from `revealForTransition` until the view is shown
  again, which covers the 2D→3D animation.
    - When building begins, any hover ends at once (`releasePointerHover`).
    - During building, presses on the canvas are dropped (`press.dropped`), the
  long-press is refused, and pointer clicks on names, actions and popped
  directory names do nothing.
    - Keyboard activation (`detail === 0`) stays live.
    - `notePointer`: capture-phase `pointermove`/`pointerover` listeners on the
  container. A `pointerover` at the same place is not a move, which is the
  trial's Chromium finding.
    - The `pointerenter` handlers on names and the actions strip are gated.
    - The wire tooltip is a hover too, so it is suppressed by the same rule.
  That was my call.
  - **`views/forceGraphRenderer.ts`.** The approved change. It is two lines
  rather than one: the optional `grabs?(): boolean` in the options interface,
  and its check in `pointerdown`. A press on a sphere while building turns the
  camera.
  - **`styles/graph.css`.**
    - While building, names and actions take no pointer.
    - At `data-building="shown"`: `cursor: progress`, plus a 2 px stroke of
  `--text-secondary` sweeping along `#graph-svg`'s top edge.
    - Under reduced motion the line is still and fainter.
    - I kept it grey, not the accent colour, because blue already means
  "offers".
  - **`persistence/local-storage.ts`, its test, and `shared/template.html`.**
    - The default is `dim`, and the select marks Dim as selected.
    - Changed expectations, each commented as a design change: the default case,
  the two "missing settings" cases, and the invalid-setting fallback.
    - A saved Glow is still kept, now asserted.
    - `tuning.ts` still has an unreachable `?? "glow"` fallback, left untouched.
  - **`tests/e2e/force-graph-building.spec.ts` (new).** Runs on this
  repository's archipelago and clicks the largest group's seed
  (`local-storage.ts`, 522 files). To make the build long enough to act inside,
  it uses existing settings only: two hops, Reveal Frames 240, wrappers off. No
  test hook.
  - **`tests/e2e/force-graph-focus.spec.ts`.** `uncover` now waits for
  `data-building` to be absent. Its first case had clicked the focused sphere
  during the arrival's build.
  - **Docs.**
    - The design paragraph in `live-documentation-explorer.mdmd.md` is dated
  November 15 and quotes the owner. It says why `data-building` is not
  `aria-busy`.

  as the default.

  history specs.

  ### How long building lasts, beside the busy column
  Headless, three runs each. Busy figures are from before the change. The 60 fps
  estimate is frames ÷ 60 plus the 0.2 s hold.

  | Action | Busy before, s here / ≈60 fps | Building, s here | Frames | ≈60 fps
  |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | Estate arrival (from page load) | 6.9 / 3.4 | 1.7 | 20 | 0.5 |
  | Estate seed click | 38.2 / 21.8 | 0.3 | 2 | 0.2–0.3 |
  | Estate Pin | 5.3 / 3.8 | 0.6 | 20 | 0.6 |
  | Estate open a directory | 12.6 / 8.0 | 0.9 | 24 | 0.6–0.9 |
  | Estate 2D→3D | 7.1 / 5.0 | 3.3 | 122 | 2.2 |
  | History sample step | 1.4 / 1.4 | 0.9 | 51 | 0.9 |
  | Repository arrival at one hop | 30.7 / 3.5 | 8.5 | 46 | 1.0 |
  | Repository 2D→3D | 23.2 / 4.6 | 5.6 | 121 | 2.2 |
  | Repository whole in Structure | over 60 / over 3.6 | 10.8 | 66 | 1.3 |

  - **Repository click on a sphere of the hop: not measured.** My setup only
  waited for building to end, so the target sphere drifted from under the
  pointer and the press landed on bare canvas. The map was not building at the
  press. This is drift after the build, as before the change.
  - **Most rows came from the build before `c6992b0f`.** That fix can only
  shorten them.

  ### Pictures, in `AI-Agent-Workspace/Screenshots/2026-11-15/building-map/`
  The `README.md` there names each file.
  - `estate-*` and `estate.webm`: the estate at defaults.
  - `repository-*` and `repository.webm`: this repository at defaults.

  `edge-whole.png`.

  ### What my own eye and hand find
  - **On the estate at defaults a seed click builds for only about 0.3 s.** The
  picture is in at once and no framing flight follows a pin, so the sign never
  appears there. The still-pointer guarantee still holds: arrived with nothing
  hovered and 0 faded. On this repository at defaults it builds for 1.0 s.
  - **The sweep is quiet.** It reads as a soft brightening travelling along the
  header's bottom border, where the map begins, and is easy to miss while
  looking at the picture. Whether that is right or too quiet is the owner's
  call.
  - **Under reduced motion the still line reads as a slightly thicker border**,
  so it says less than the sweep does.
  - **The wait cursor can't be pictured headless.** The spec reads it as
  `progress` over the canvas.
  - **The spec never pressed during a short build.** In the build it uses, which
  lasts 6–20 s, presses were refused and the map never looked broken. Because
  building is about 0.2–1 s on most real actions, I expect a refused click to
  read as "too early" rather than "broken". That is a guess: no hand has tried
  it.
  - **One behaviour change to weigh.** After a build, a pointer that hasn't
  moved no longer re-hovers when the camera wheels or Fits beneath it until it
  moves. That is the rule as decided.

  ### The stillness tail (reported, not changed)
  - **Where it lives.**
  `packages/explorer/src/client/views/forceGraphLayout.ts`:
    - `STILL_FRACTION = 3e-5` and `QUIET_TICKS = 10` at lines 347–351;
    - the test itself at lines 535–544.

    The view turns `onStill` into `simulationRunning = false` once the reveal is
  whole, in `forceGraphView.ts` near line 1604.
  - **What it waits for.** The tether is holding, no sphere is held by a hand,
  and the largest move in a tick has stayed at or under 3×10⁻⁵ of the picture's
  diagonal for ten ticks in a row. On the estate that is a few hundredths of a
  unit, far below a pixel.
  - **What I think keeps it running (not measured).** Either:
    - a slow creep of a loosely held body, such as another group's seed held
  only by weakened gravity and the 0.02 tether; or
    - a sub-pixel limit cycle from the approximated repulsion (Approximation
  0.3) as spheres cross octree opening thresholds, which keeps restarting the
  ten quiet ticks.
  - **How to tell them apart.** Log each tick's largest move against the
  diagonal, with which sphere made it, and repeat the click at Approximation 0.

● Directory handles are on your port 8999 now. Try the client directory
  (http://127.0.0.1:8999/?view=local&dir=packages/explorer/src/client): press
  Zoom Out seven times, click anywhere on the history box to open it, then click
  its name to close it.

  What changed, with the designer's reason for each:

  - The whole closed box is the button. At the zoom where a directory is the
    thing to act on, the target should be as large as possible; that is Fitts's
    law (the smaller a target, the longer it takes to hit).
  - A chevron replaces the word "Open" and the close cross. The chevron is the
    sign that tells you the box opens, the same one a file tree uses. One sign
    for one act, and no words of ours.
  - A directory's name is never smaller than its files' names. It was about 6 px
    tall beside 13 px file names at the far zoom. The parent path is drawn
    muted and gives way first; the directory's own name is bright and is cut
    last.
  - Names are whole. "bootst…" and "persiste…" now fit, by coming down a little
    in size before breaking inside a word.

  Two things that are not right yet:

  - Inside a large membrane the handle is off screen. It sits at the membrane's
    top-left corner, so with a file as the subject and the camera in the middle
    of its directory (the picture you reported from) there is still nothing to
    press until you pan or press Fit. My proposal is a handle that stays in view
    for each directory you are inside, the way VS Code's sticky scroll keeps
    the enclosing scopes at the top of the editor. You have said breadcrumbs
    were "never good enough", and this is close kin, so I'd build it as a trial
    for you to accept or reject. Not started.
  - A closed box has an empty row at reading size where "Open" used to be. The
    cells take that row in the next milestone, which the builder has started.

  The directory round is committed (e4415dba). One test is red on main, and it
  is my doing from this afternoon, not this round's: the 3D-cue change put the
  layout lab's model out of step with the page on one scope. The fix is written
  and its check is running.

  ## Forks I decided
  1. Tab rise is constant across looks; only the width follows the held name.
  Where the held name leaves no room for a step, the folder is drawn as a plain
  rounded box.
  2. A skin's own cells stand in one row (two staggered rows do not fit inside a
  13-unit skin). Caps: 6 cells in a skin, 3 in a skin inside it, 3 skins inside
  a skin.
  3. A directory with no file directly in it is an empty skin, not a skin with
  an invented cell.
  4. A far-look row no taller than a cell draws no skin; it shows cells and a
  veil.
  5. Three presses out, the held row of cells may use the box down to 2 units
  above its foot, so that a 13-unit row fits.

  ## Forks handed back
  - **Colour.** By kind, a source file's cell is the 3D map's blue and a test's
  is green — the same hues the 2D legend beside them uses for Offers and Uses.
  The trial kept the cells grey for that reason. By kind tells more, but as the
  2D map stands my eye prefers grey; `sheet-colours-segoe.png` shows both. There
  is also no 2D legend entry for kinds.
  - **Tab in the far looks.** Three presses out, narrow boxes get a short step
  or none; seven presses out, few boxes have one, so siblings differ. A tab as
  tall as the held name is the alternative; I did not build it.
  - **Round with small contents.** A skin around one cell is a ring around a
  dot, and an empty skin is a hollow ring: close to the marks the owner could
  not read. In Tab the same things read as a small folder.

● Here is your colour clash and two ways out of it, side by side: three palettes
  on scripts (AI-Agent-Workspace/Screenshots/2026-11-15/kind-palette/probe-thre
  e-palettes.png). Left is what you saw tonight; the wires are the same in all
  three. It is a probe: I recoloured the cells in the builder's build by hand.
  Nothing is built yet, and the 3D map isn't shown.

  Why they clash. Designers call hue a visual variable: like size or position,
  it can carry one code per picture. The 2D map already spends blue and green on
  a connection's role, so a second code needs hues of its own. Measured,
  implementation's blue sits 12.7 from the "offers" blue and the test's green
  14.3 from the "uses" green, on a scale where 15 is the floor for telling two
  colours apart at a glance.

  What I found while looking:
  - Only three file kinds exist (implementation, test, asset), plus documents.
  - The product colours them three different ways that nobody designed together:
    the 3D map (implementation blue, tests green), the 2D cards (tests violet:
    the halo around steps.mjs and its TEST chip in the picture), and the details
    panel, where two of the three colours were never defined.
  - In today's 3D map, green tests and orange directory hubs look nearly the
    same to a red-green colour-blind reader (2.5, where 8 is the target).

  My proposal: one palette for file kinds, defined once, used by both maps, and
  never blue or green. Tests become violet everywhere, as the 2D cards already
  have them. For implementation there are two candidates:
  - A, amber (my pick). The cells read as a second code at once, amber and
    violet sit far apart on the colour wheel so a directory's mix is plain at a
    glance, and every colour stays clear of the wires.
  - B, neutral. The calmest; tests are the only colour. It gives up most of the
    splash you liked, and at the cells' strength a grey dot and a faded green
    wire come close.

  Either one changes the 3D map's main colour from blue to amber or pale grey.
  That is too big to settle from a probe, so nothing changes yet: after the
  directory round lands, a builder makes the palettes switchable live in both
  maps and you choose there. If one of the two is already a clear no from the
  picture, say so and only the other gets built.

  The legend. You're right, and I was about to make it worse: minutes before
  your message I had asked the builder to add rows to it to explain the cell
  colours. That is withdrawn. Instead, hovering a cell shows that file's name,
  so the reader's own files explain the colours (steps.test.ts under a violet
  dot). Cutting the legend's wording goes in the same round as the palette, and
  I'll show you a before and after.

  Still waiting: the 3D "building" round's one failed test is queued for a rerun
  behind the builders' browser runs, and the three directory shapes arrive on
  your port when the builder hands back.

❯ I'm looking at probe-three-palettes and what occurs to me is that these
  probes do not attempt to make consistent the coloring of these nodes inside
  the directories in 2D and 3D. Indeed, that was half my point: there is a

  implementations). We need substantially more to even remotely confidently say

  views.

  respect to understandability of what's inside the directories, and "Today:
  implementation blue, tests green" is the most aesthetically pleasing based on
  the surrounding screen area (consistency with onscreen colors despite
  conflict of color meaning).

  ## Handback: the fewest returns (base 3f734433, branch
  worktree-agent-a0e2d2f2ceebf9110)

  **Commits** (none amended, nothing pushed):
  - 49873586 Stand a cycle's files in the order that returns the fewest
  references (code, tests, Live Docs, Explorer design sentence, the lab's
  Pretext path)
  - 9e7d480a Record why … (the decisions log entry)
  - 4d317e29 Wrap the cycle order's long comment lines
  - aa76fcf1 Keep the evidence … (pictures and measurements)

  **What changed**
  - New `packages/explorer/src/client/views/localView/cycle-order.ts`, with its
  test file. It holds:
    - `comparePrices`, the one price: fewest references backward (pair lines
  count by `linksOf`), then fewest pairs with a return, then fewest
  *reference-joined* pairs turned from the old greedy order.
      - Pairs that no reference joins are not counted, so the price depends only
  on which references return.
      - I call the old order "the greedy order" in code and docs, not "today's",
  so a later reader knows what it means.
    - `exactOrder`: a search over subsets, exact because every part of the price
  is additive over pairs.
    - `greedyOrder`: Eades–Lin–Smyth, either unweighted (the old rule, kept as
  the tie-break) or weighted by references.
    - `improvedOrder`: best single-file move until no move lowers the price.
    - `cycleOrder`: exact search up to `EXACT_LIMIT = 12`, otherwise the
  weighted greedy order improved by moves.
  - `branches.ts` / `rankBranches`:
    - Builds a reference-weight matrix once and calls `cycleOrder` per
  component.
    - `back` and the simplex constraints now come from one `returns()` test.
    - I removed a dead `forward` map that was built but never read.
  - `scripts/layout-lab/capture.ts`: Pretext is found where Node resolves
  `@chenglou/pretext/package.json` (a one-line change), so the lab captures from
  a worktree.
  - Docs:
    - Decisions log entry "A Cycle's Files Stand in the Order That Returns the
  Fewest References", with the measurement and the costs.
    - One sentence where the Explorer design describes how cycles are broken.
    - Authored Purpose/Notes for `cycle-order.ts` and its test, plus notes in
  the Live Docs of `branches.ts`, `branches.test.ts` and `capture.ts` (all cite
  #turn-5).
    - Live Docs regenerated.

  ## Second milestone: a return is one term of the ranking's price

  Your decision is built: one price in the ranking's own unit, a Return Cost
  lever, default 0.25. The default separates the pictures that only score better
  from most that read better. Two pictures stay on the wrong side whatever the
  cost: `adapters/index.ts` (worse) and `go.ts` (better but not flipped).
  Separately, three large pictures return more than today at the default, though
  by eye they read better. That fork is yours; details below.

  **Branch commits:** 88a742cc (ranking, lever, lab fix, slopcop fix), 13bcac48
  (merge of main at `efbda787`), ea4f30ac (decisions log), f80a8ea3 (evidence).
  - Main did not touch `branches.ts`. The directory builder's change was not in
  main yet, so there were no `branches.ts` hunks to resolve.
  - The only conflicts were in two Live Docs: `layout-presets.ts.mdmd.md` (I
  kept main's "Files only" paragraph and my `returnCost` paragraph) and
  `types.ts.mdmd.md` (generated parts only; I took main's and regenerated).

  ### What I built
  - **The price** (`rankBranches` in `branches.ts`): every reference costs the
  columns it spans in the picture, forward or back, weighted as `linksOf` does.
  A returning reference costs `returnCost` more on top. No other term is added.
    - The column solve is now its own function, `rankColumns`. The ranking
  computes it once per candidate.
    - Each cycle, in turn and with the others held, keeps its cheapest
  candidate. The greedy order wins ties.
  - **Candidates** (`cycleCandidates` in `cycle-order.ts`): the greedy order
  first; then each order a single move reaches on the way to the fewest-returns
  order, with the leftmost differing file moved first; then the fewest-returns
  order itself. Each set of returns is offered once.
    - The fewest-returns order is the first milestone's search, renamed
  `fewestReturnsOrder`.
    - Across the repository's 975 pictures: 942 have no candidate beyond the
  greedy order, 27 have one, 5 have two, 1 has three.
  - **Time:** the worst picture is `core.ts` (25-file cycle), up from 1.55 to
  2.84 ms. `branch-renderer.ts` went from 1.11 to 1.32 ms. `client/types.ts` (53
  files) dropped from 3.37 to 2.56 ms. No bound was needed.

  ### How the default was chosen
  I took the least cost at which each of the 34 changed pictures leaves the
  greedy order, and set it beside the lab's signals and my own reading. Readings
  come from contact sheets of both pictures after Fit, greedy against the
  fewest-returns order.

  | picture | leaves greedy at | my reading |
  |---|---|---|
  | Processor.cs, Record.cs, compiler-edges.json, helpers.rs,
  directory-ancestry.ts, manifest.ts, forceGraphScope.ts, forceGraphLayout.ts,
  html.ts | 0 | better |
  | branch-scene.ts | 0 (to an order with +2 returns) | better |
  | directory-flow.ts, branch-renderer.ts | 0.25 (at 0 an order with +2 / +1) |
  better |
  | go.ts | 2 | better (but see below) |
  | css.ts, aspnet.ts, csharp.ts | 0 | mixed |
  | core.ts, index.ts | 0 (to an order on the way, not the fewest) | mixed for
  the fewest order; index.ts worse at the default, see below |
  | c.ts 8, json.ts 8, forceGraphReveal.ts 4 | — | mixed |
  | ModelFactory.cs, quantity.rs, item.rs, memory.rs 0.25; ProcessorConfig.cs,
  force-graph-reveal.spec.ts 2.5; controller.ts 16; forceGraphView.ts 0 | — |
  neutral |
  | branches.ts | 0 (+2 at every cost up to 20) | mixed |
  | localView/ directory | 0 (+1 at every cost up to 20) | mixed |
  | **dotnetConfig.ts** | **0.5** | only scores better |
  | **sourceAnalysis.ts** | **20** | only scores better |
  | **powershell.ts** | **above 20** | only scores better |

  At 0.25 all three "only scores better" pictures keep the greedy order, and 12
  of the 13 "better" ones have flipped. The full table with lab signals is
  `AI-Agent-Workspace/Probes/2026-11-15/fewest-returns-2/flip-table.md`.

  ### What is still wrong
  1. **`adapters/index.ts` is on the wrong side whatever the cost.** At cost 0
  it already moves to an order on the way (25 returns where it had 37, 4 columns
  where it had 6).
     - The lab says it is clearly worse: 84% more wire, crossings 151 to 650,
  47% taller.
     - By eye too: its adapters stand in one column of 18 (the fullest held 13),
  so the fitted picture is a narrow tower too small to read.
     - The ranking cannot see card heights. I tried a price per card in the
  fullest column (measured, not added); it only moves the picture to the first
  milestone's order, which is also worse. Only the placement knows vertical wire
  length.
  2. **`go.ts` reads better in the fewest-returns order but only flips at 2**,
  past `dotnetConfig.ts` at 0.5. The difference is again the cards' heights.
  3. **Three pictures return more than the greedy order at the default**, at
  every cost up to 16 or 20. An order on the way can return all of one file's
  references so it stands where its returns are short; the ring-of-three unit
  case shows the same thing.
     - `branch-scene.ts` draws 6 returns where it drew 4.
     - `branches.ts` returns 2 more, but they are on hidden rows, so it still
  draws 4.
     - The `localView` directory draws 11 where it drew 10.
     - By eye all three read better: narrower, shorter wires. So I kept your
  candidate set as specified.
  4. **Two options measured, not adopted. Both are yours to decide:**
     - **Only offer orders that return no more than the greedy order.** No
  picture then returns more. `branch-scene.ts` and the directory take the
  fewest-returns order at 0. `branches.ts` keeps the greedy order up to 5.
  Nothing else changes.
     - **A price per column (5 per column).** `css.ts` holds the greedy order up
  to 4. `aspnet.ts` and `dotnetConfig.ts` hold it up to 1.5, which widens the
  safe band from 0.25–0.5 to 0.25–1.5. `go.ts` would then need 5.

  ### Four pictures at the default
  - `branch-renderer.ts` (drawn returns 8 → 6): reads better. `controller.ts`
  now stands to its right, the fan into the subject is gathered, and the long
  wires are gone. The two small cards past the subject look a little stranded.
  - `manifest.ts` (1 → 0): reads better. Four columns instead of six, one column
  of adapters, and the 3D button goes out.
  - `core.ts` (7 → 6): mixed. It takes an order on the way rather than the
  fewest-returns one: far fewer crossings, but a tall column shrinks the fitted
  picture.
  - `powershell.ts` (2 → 2): unchanged, as intended.

  ### The lever
  - **Where:** Tuning → 2D Layout → "Return Cost", between Width Cost and
  Membrane Simplicity. Slider 0–20, step 0.25, default 0.25.
  - **Its description:** "Higher reads fewer connections backward through a
  cycle, at the price of longer wires; zero weighs one as a wire."
  - **Remembered** with the saved tuning, and **carried** by shared links and
  exported layouts. A link made before this keeps the viewer's own setting.
  - The page's ranking and the lab's `LabConfig` both read it. Picture of the
  slider: `return-cost-lever.png`.

  ### The lab's drift: fixed
  The fault was in the measurer's rule, not in your mend's idea. For a reference
  end with no row of its own, the page's `getAnchor` puts the **using** end on
  the Internals row's dot, which the disclosure can hide; only the offering end
  goes on the always-visible hub. The lab put both on the hub, so it counted a
  return at `core.ts` that the page doesn't draw. That was the 20 px.
  - Proof: I mended `endShown` in `scripts/layout-lab/measurer.ts`. The lab then
  agrees with the first milestone's page, with no drift, on all seven pictures
  that had drifted. Five of those had already drifted at base, so this cures an
  older drift too.
  - On this build, after merging main, the lab agrees with the page on the 4
  deck scopes and all 33 changed files.

› Message from general-purpose
  ## Third milestone: stopped at a fork. The placed price works, but its time is
  far over budget

  **What I found:** a default Return Cost does separate the readings under the
  placed price. But choosing a cycle's order on the first draw costs 0.2 to 2.5
  s on the large pictures, and cutting candidates cannot bring that near 50 ms.
  A single exact placement of `branch-renderer.ts` or `branches.ts` takes
  170–320 ms here, on the nominal model or a page-like one. I stopped before the
  tests, docs and browser batch, because how the time is bounded changes what
  gets built.

  **Where the work stands:** nothing committed this leg. Main had not moved
  since `efbda787`. Uncommitted in the worktree, type-checking clean apart from
  the old ranking cases:
  - `branches.ts`: `rankBranches` no longer chooses. It stands each cycle in the
  order given in `RankingOptions.orders` (keyed by the cycle's files and
  internal references), else the greedy order. It returns every cycle's
  candidates as `ranking.cycles`. The span price and `returnCost` are gone from
  it.
  - `cycle-choice.ts` (new, pure): `pictureCost` and `chooseCycleOrders`.
    - The price is the page's `scoreOf` from its own starts on the nominal
  model, churn aside. On top of that come the wires' horizontal length (provider
  column's right edge to consumer column's left edge, weighted as the placement
  weighs vertical) and Return Cost × returning links.
    - Each cycle chooses in turn; the greedy order keeps a tie. A cycle already
  known keeps its order.
  - `branch-renderer.ts`: `explorationOf` explores, then chooses. The choice is
  cached per (cycle key, filters, tuning), so a pin that adds no file or
  reference to a cycle never re-prices it. It writes `data-cycle-choice-ms` and
  `data-cycle-choices` to the map's root element. I also extracted
  `sceneTuningOf` / `startCostsOf` so the choice and the drawing share one
  source.
  - The lab's `evaluate` makes the same choice through the same function.
  - The default is now 1500 px; the slider runs 0–5000 px by 25.

  **Not yet touched:** the ranking and cycle tests (they still pass
  `returnCost`), the new choice tests, docs, Live Docs, the browser batch.

  ### Flip table with the placed price (default tuning, pixels of wire)
  Columns: least Return Cost at which the picture leaves the greedy order;
  returns/columns for greedy and at 1500 (F = fewest-returns order, o = an order
  on the way, + = more returns than greedy); and my reading of the same
  pictures from the second milestone's sheets.

  | picture | leaves at | greedy | at 1500 | reading |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | Processor.cs, Record.cs, compiler-edges.json, helpers.rs,
  directory-ancestry.ts, forceGraphScope.ts, forceGraphLayout.ts | 0 | | F |
  better |
  | branches.ts | 0 | 8/10 | 6/11 F | mixed |
  | directory-flow.ts | 0 (more returns below ~970) | 8/8 | 6/8 F | better |
  | branch-renderer.ts | 0 (more returns below ~1390) | 10/10 | 7/10 F | better
  |
  | localView/ directory | 0 (more returns below ~1070) | 10/11 | 7/11 F | mixed
  |
  | branch-scene.ts | 0 | 4/9 | 6/7 + (to ~15,400) | better |
  | controller.ts | 0 | 6/9 | 5/9 F | neutral |
  | forceGraphView.ts | 0 | 13/10 | 9/9 F | neutral |
  | core.ts | 750 | 30/9 | 21/6 o | mixed |
  | index.ts | **0** | 37/6 | 22/5 F | mixed by eye; lab says worse |
  | memory.rs 225, item.rs 250, quantity.rs 250, force-graph-reveal.spec.ts 525,
  ProcessorConfig.cs 1075 | as listed | | F | neutral |
  | html.ts | 575 | 5/4 | 3/4 F | better |
  | csharp.ts | 800 | 7/6 | 3/5 F | mixed |
  | manifest.ts | 825 | 9/6 | 6/4 F | better |
  | **go.ts** | **1475** | 4/4 | 2/5 F | better |
  | aspnet.ts | 1525 | 5/4 | greedy | mixed |
  | css.ts | 1575 | 3/3 | greedy | mixed |
  | ModelFactory.cs | 1600 | 2/6 | greedy | neutral |
  | forceGraphReveal.ts | 1900 | 13/4 | greedy | mixed |
  | **dotnetConfig.ts** | **2675** | 7/4 | greedy | only scores better |
  | **sourceAnalysis.ts, powershell.ts** | **>3000** | | greedy | only scores
  better |
  | c.ts, json.ts | >3000 | | greedy | mixed |

  **A default exists.** Anything from 1475 up to 2675 flips all thirteen
  pictures that read better and keeps all three that only score better. I set
  1500, the low end, because it moves the fewest mixed pictures. At 1500, 25 of
  the 34 pictures change and 9 keep the greedy order.

  **Your expectations, checked:**
  - `go.ts` flips with the better pictures — **true**.
  - powershell, dotnetConfig and sourceAnalysis stay — **true**.
  - A picture returns more than today only where the wire it saves outweighs the
  returns it adds — **true**, and at 1500 that is only `branch-scene.ts`: it
  saves 30,856 px for 2 × 1500 px of returns.
  - `adapters/index.ts` keeps the greedy order — **false.** The tower (25
  returns, 4 columns) is refused: it prices about 28,000 px above greedy. But
  the fewest-returns picture (22 returns, 5 columns) is about 54,000 px cheaper
  than greedy on the nominal model, so it is chosen at every cost, even 0.
    - By eye it is mixed: it is index.ts alone at the left, then a column of
  about 6 cards and a column of about 17 adapters, against greedy's 13.
    - The lab, with real card sizes, called that picture worse in the first
  milestone (+58% wire, crossings ×2).
    - It is on the wrong side if you read it as worse. The nominal model, every
  card 180 px tall, does not see what the real card sizes do.

  ### Time: why this is a fork
  The choice's own time on a fresh draw, median of 7 runs, as specified (3
  starts per candidate, every candidate):

  | picture | choice time | cut to 1 start | cut to 1 start, greedy + fewest
  only |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | core.ts (37 cards, 3 candidates) | 583 ms | 333 | 208 |
  | client/types.ts | 0 (no candidates) | | |
  | branch-renderer.ts (25 cards, 5 candidates) | 1673 ms | 741 | 583 |
  | adapters/index.ts | 151 ms | 82 | 67 |
  | branches.ts (34 cards, 4 candidates) | 2536 ms | 856 | 749 |
  | localView/ directory (72 cards, 5 candidates) | 312 ms | 155 | 128 |

  The machine was busy with other builders' browsers throughout. The time is the
  exact placement, not the candidate count: `branch-renderer.ts` has 551
  placement wires (lane slots included). The page's own first draw already pays
  that placement once per start.

  ### Options for you
  - **A.** Accept it: 0.2–2.5 s more on the first draw of a large cyclic
  picture, cached for the rest of the session.
  - **B.** Bound it: one start per candidate, and only the greedy and
  fewest-returns orders. The worst becomes about 0.6–0.75 s (branch-renderer,
  branches), and `branch-scene.ts` and `core.ts` lose their in-between orders.
  - **C. (my recommendation)** Draw the greedy order first, and let the existing
  continuing search price the candidates in idle time and move to a cheaper
  one, as it already moves pictures after arrival. Cached thereafter, so the
  second visit draws the chosen order at once. The cost: on a first visit the
  returns, the cards' feet and the 3D cue change once, shortly after arrival.
  - **D.** Price on a cheaper model than the exact placement. That brings back a
  proxy, which your decision moved away from.

  ### One more consequence to weigh
  Because the choice is cached per cycle (so a pin elsewhere never flips it), a
  cycle is priced in the picture where the session first met it. A link opened
  fresh prices it in the link's own picture, so a session that pinned its way
  there could, rarely, show a different order than the link it shares.

  batch (lab agreement, the four pictures, registry and go.ts side by side, the
  specs), and commits.

  - `choice-time*.ts` with their `.txt` outputs
  - `profile*.ts`

  ## 2. The widow nit: more than one line's change, left alone

  - **The existing rule:** `chooseNameHeader` (`held-lines.ts`) already keeps an
  extension with the piece before it when the same number of lines carries it
  and nothing is cut.
  - **Why it did not apply:** in "controller / .ts" and "Payment / .cs" the last
  word with its extension is wider than a line at the look's size, and the name
  has no other seam. The only break there is before the extension.
  - **The only cure is a smaller size.** `filesNameScale` is fed the widest of
  `heldSegments`, where the extension is its own piece (`card-reading.ts`, about
  line 597). Feeding it `seamsOf`'s pieces is one line, but it shrinks every
  card whose last word plus extension is its widest piece. That trades a widow
  for smaller names, so I did not build it. This is from reading the code; I did
  not try it in the browser.

  ## 8. Which value my eye would make the default

  Hidden, as built. If the owner wants one of the three, the middle cut is the
  candidate, after its fallback is decided (section 10).

  - On the owner's card the two "always" values turn a ragged ladder of one- and
  two-line names into fourteen even pairs and use the card's room. The prose
  tone alone keeps the eye on the names.

  and drops the ends that differ. `parseDocumentationAnchors` and
  `ParseDocumentationAnchorsOptions` become "parseDocumentationA…" and
  "ParseDocumentationA…" under Segoe's lines.

    - On `branch-renderer.ts` both draw `refreshReadingPresentation` and
  `refreshReadingGeometry` as the same "refreshReading…".

  on-pr…", a plain loss.

  `branch-renderer.ts` (4 of 6 kept), but keeps 2 of 14 on the owner's card,
  which is what the owner disliked.

  ## 9. What the middle cut costs in reading a name at a glance

  - The name is put together, not read: the eye takes two pieces and supplies
  the gap.
  - A word sought in the middle ("Link", "Document") is not found by eye.
  - The name's length and outline, by which a known name is recognised without
  reading, are gone.
  - One card shows two kinds of cut, because a seam cut does not always fit; an
  ellipsis at a name's end no longer says whether the name ends there.

  ## 10. Forks

  **Handed back: what the middle cut does when the first and last pieces do not
  fit together.** The brief is silent on this.
  - (a) As built: cut at the end. It breaks only at seams, but gives up the
  name's end exactly where it matters (the `refreshReading…` twins; three of the
  owner's twelve).
  - (b) Cut inside the first piece and keep the last whole: "ref…Presentation",
  "Documentatio…Violation". I built this first and removed it; it breaks inside
  a word and read worse to me.
  - (c) A leading ellipsis with the last pieces whole: "…Presentation",
  "…LinkViolation". It breaks only at seams and always keeps the end; the
  product already draws a leading ellipsis on directory paths ("…ipts/slopcop").
  Not built.
  - I would take (c) if the middle value is kept.

  **Decided by me, say if you disagree:**
  - In the two "always" values a described row keeps its line before a folded
  name keeps its second line.
  - The file's line is drawn in the kept lines' type (11px, weight 400, prose
  tone), not the Purpose's own.
  - The tooltip is the label's `title`.

  ## 11. Deviation from the brief, and what still looks wrong

  - **Wire ends move between Hidden and a value.** The brief asks both that a
  kept line count in its row's head for even spacing and that no wire end move.
  The dots stand with the heads, so they move: on the owner's card three presses
  out, by up to about twenty screen pixels (read off the sheet: the fourth
  row's dots at 199 under Hidden and at 220 with a line always kept). No box and
  no row's place moves. The spec holds boxes, row places and which wires exist,
  not wire ends.
  - **The folded name that gives up its second line** (half-folded card) is cut
  by the row's own ellipsis at its end, also in the middle value, and I set no
  tooltip for it.
  - **"First line, where room" keeps no row line on the half-folded card,**
  since kept lines give way first, though the file's line still shows.
  - **A card whose header is too short for the file's line shows no sign that a
  line is missing.**

› Message from general-purpose
  ## Fourth leg: a cycle's order is now a trial of the continuing search

  **Summary:** option C is built and verified. The first draw is `main`'s. The
  search then tries each cycle's other orders on the page's own measurements,
  and the cue and the cards' feet follow when it moves. A default separates the
  readings: **1500 px**. Three things stay wrong: `adapters/index.ts` does not
  keep the greedy order, `branches.ts` is judged differently by the page and the
  lab, and `branch-renderer.ts` drew first slower in one measurement.

  **Commits on the branch:**
  - 30c16ccc: the search's cycle trials
  - 2c72138d: merge of main at `dd3eec62`
  - 383dec5d: an endpoint fix found in the browser
  - ce660d7c: docs
  - 8b79406b: evidence

  Main has not moved since. Nothing pushed, nothing amended.

  ### What I built
  - **First draw.** Each cycle stands in the greedy order, or in the order this
  session's search last adopted for it. Nothing is priced. The nominal choice
  and `data-cycle-choice-ms` are gone.
  - **Ranking.** `rankBranches` stands a cycle in the order given and offers the
  others (`cycles`: key, candidates, standing order).
  - **`cycle-trials.ts` (new, pure; it replaces the second leg's
  `cycle-choice.ts`).**
    - The two added terms: `horizontalWire` (provider column's right edge to
  consumer column's left edge, weighted as the placement weighs vertical wire)
  and `rankingTerms` (plus Return Cost × returning links).
    - `cycleOrdersOf` / `nextCycleTrial`: every offered order except the one
  standing when the search begins, each tried once.
    - `walkCycleTrials`: the walk the lab and the tests share.
  - **The search.**
    - Trial order: a cycle's orders come first, then seeds and joins in turn
  (`nextTrialKind`). A cycle's order changes the columns every other trial
  orders within, so the seeds then refine what stands.
    - Pricing: a trial is ordered from the shown picture's own order and laid
  out on the cached measurements. Its price is the page's `scoreOf` plus the two
  terms, and those terms are added to every price the search compares.
    - Judgment: the trial is judged by `judgeAlignment`, so it spends no seed
  and no patience. If adopted, the cards' feet are redressed for its returns
  (`dressReturns`) and it is measured on the page, then moved to; if refused,
  the feet are restored.
    - Session memory: an adopted order goes into the session memory, keyed by
  the cycle's files and internal references.
    - With `searchStarts: 0` nothing is tried.
  - **Shared and changed pieces.**
    - `sceneTuningOf` and `startCostsOf` are shared by the first draw and the
  search.
    - The adopted picture's `data-order-score` now includes the two terms, so it
  matches its prediction `data-search-priced`.
    - The lab's `evaluate` reports the first draw and, beside it, `cycleSearch`
  (the same walk on the capture's card model). `refine.ts` stops at a cycle
  trial.
  - **Endpoint fix found in the browser (383dec5d).** The search's row cache
  only measured endpoints of forward wires. A cycle trial that turns a return
  forward threw inside the idle callback, and the search never settled. Every
  reference between two files is now measured. The two browser cases that timed
  out on the first sitting were that bug's red.
  - **The lever.** Tuning → 2D Layout → **Return Cost**, 0–5000 px by 25,
  default 1500, remembered and carried by links and exports. Its sentence: "How
  many pixels of wire one connection read backward through a cycle is worth;
  higher reads fewer backward."
