  ### What was built
  - **`closedFamily` in `directory-handle.ts`** (pure, 7 new Vitest cases): the
  closed boxes of one parent get one scale (the tightest's), More in all or
  none, the cells' row in all or none. `siblingScale` **mirrors**
  `pinnedRowScales` rather than sharing it (that function's rows carry a pinned
  flag a box has no use for).
  - **`closedBoxWrites`** now reads every closed box at once and groups by
  parent path.
  - **More in all or in none is my reading of item 2.** Applied box by box, the
  scan root would have More on 4 of 6 boxes, which breaks your own principle.
  - **Why the estate's label was one column wide:** `stripColumns` is only
  computed for pictures placed from the top (an opened directory in Flow). A
  picture placed by its wires has no rows, so the strip is kept over the first
  column alone and the label stopped there whatever stood beside it.
  - **The mend, `handleReach`** (replaces `handleRun`): beyond the strip's
  columns the handle runs over leading columns that are level with the first and
  hold no card, closed box or inner membrane within the strip's height, read
  from the pose. No layout number changes. The other way, reserving the strip in
  more columns, would change every pin picture's layout; not done.
  - **Fit before cut** is in the stylesheet: `--dir-scale` comes down to `room /
  (name + controls)`, floored at `--dir-floor` (`HANDLE_FLOOR`, the connections
  size, now the one floor for closed and opened names). The renderer writes
  `--dir-name`.
  - **Found by the audit and mended:** under Segoe metrics an opened handle's
  name lost a pixel of descender at the far looks, on `90bcd106` too. Path and
  name now clip across only.
  - **The design doc's handle paragraph stood twice, word for word** (from the
  squash); one copy removed.

  **Outside my named lane:** `branch-renderer.ts`, label code only (imports, one
  `--dir-floor` line in `labelFor`, one `removeProperty` in `fillLabel`,
  `writeCountNeed` → `writeHandleNeeds`, four lines in the pose loop for the
  reach). Not touched: `branches.ts`, the lab, `reading.css`, `ladder-rooms.ts`,
  Tuning.

  ### 1. `branch-renderer.ts`'s slower first draw came from a different picture,
  not slower code
  **Method.** Ten draws of each picture on each build, plus one unrecorded
  warm-up. Builds were interleaved and their order reversed every round. Search
  off, 1440×900, reading `data-layout-ms` (ranking, cards, starts and
  placement). Three builds:
  - **main-own:** main's build (28b7f224) on main's own graph.
  - **main-same:** the same build given this branch's `explorer-data.json`, so
  both draw the same picture.
  - **mine:** this branch's final build.

  Medians and ranges, in ms:

  | Picture | mine | main-same | main-own |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `branch-renderer.ts` | 1,565 (1,444–1,909) | 1,664 (1,395–2,192) | 1,243
  (1,158–1,856) |
  | `branches.ts` | 1,240 (1,091–1,686) | 1,203 (1,067–1,453) | 1,195
  (1,085–1,659) |
  | `adapters/index.ts` | 523 (467–843) | 514 (466–817) | 567 (475–1,074) |

  **The cause.** On the same data the two builds are equal within the noise. The
  leg-4 gap was the picture: main's own graph lacks this round's
  `cycle-order.ts` and `cycle-trials.ts`. So `branch-renderer.ts` uses 24 files
  there and 26 here; `branches.ts` has 14 out / 19 in there and 15 / 21 here.

  **The ranking alone.** Timed in Node on the same graph, main's module against
  mine: 16.7 vs 14.4 ms, 16.2 vs 15.6 ms, 12.9 vs 15.2 ms.

  **Your suspect, the endpoint fix (383dec5d).** It was never on the first
  draw's clock: `layoutMs` is written before `rowsOf`. It was still DOM reading
  at first paint, so I made it lazy as you suggested. The first draw now
  measures only the drawn wires' endpoints. `cycleStep` measures every
  reference's endpoints, returning ones included, when it first tries a cycle's
  order.

  **Case.** `return-cost.spec.ts` now reads `data-search-endpoints`: "drawn"
  with the search off, "every" once the search has tried a cycle.

  ### 2. On one graph the page and the lab's walk agree on every trial of all 33
  pictures
  **Why they seemed to disagree.** The leg-4 lab walk used captures taken before
  this round's own files joined the graph; the page drew the graph after. On
  one graph the verdicts agree on all 40 trials across the 33 pictures, with
  drift 0 on every first draw.
  - 31 trials are priced alike to the pixel.
  - 9 differ by 3–22 px. The difference is in the vertical placement, which the
  page predicts from endpoints cached on the shown cards; in three pictures it
  is also 1 px of a card's height.
  - The narrowest verdict's margin is 150 px (`css.ts`), so no residual comes
  near a verdict.

  **`branches.ts`, term by term (today's graph).** The first picture's price is
  1,469,735.

  | Term | Trial 1, lab and page | Trial 2, lab | Trial 2, page |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | vertical | 287,398 | 213,847 | 213,825 |
  | crossings | 8,335 | 7,681 | 7,681 |
  | height | 3,971 | 3,739 | 3,739 |
  | width | 5,766 | 6,383 | 6,383 |
  | churn | 6 | 5 | 5 |
  | unevenness | 4,425 | 4,075 | 4,075 |
  | steps | 25 | 29 | 29 |
  | unlevel | 13,029 | 8,602 | 8,602 |
  | horizontal wire | 482,722 | 558,160 | 558,160 |
  | returning links | 10 | 6 | 6 |
  | price | 1,540,513 | 1,477,251 | 1,477,229 |

  Both trials are refused on both sides.

  **Churn.** Both sides count churn the same way, with `signalsOf` against the
  shown picture's tops (6 and 5 above).

  **The feet, done as you suggested.** `footedHeights` finds the cards whose
  line on returning connections a trial adds or removes. It adds or subtracts
  one foot's room, which `footRoom` measures once per search by probing a card
  with the page's own element.
  - Without it, 18 of the 40 trials were predicted off the walk, by up to 1,200
  px. On `core.ts` the trials were predicted a foot taller than they stand
  (5,156 vs 5,136).
  - `branches.ts` trial 2 was 440 px off without it, all in the vertical
  placement.
  - No verdict changes on today's graph either way.

  **Cases added**, in `layout-lab.spec.ts`, sharing a helper `cycleTrials`. The
  lab's `CycleSearch.tried` now carries each trial's terms (`CycleTerms`) for
  this.
  - **`core.ts`, term by term:** every term of the page's prediction equals the
  walk's. It was red on a build without the feet (height 5,156 vs 5,136, plus
  unlevel and vertical) and green on the final build.
  - **`branches.ts`:** the verdicts and every term except the vertical placement
  equal the walk's. It holds the agreement, not the feet fix: it also passes
  without the feet, because there the feet move only the vertical term.

› Message from general-purpose
  ## Motion-rest round: measurement handed back before building

  I stopped before building anything. The brief said to hand the numbers back if
  they showed something neither candidate predicts, and they do. The long tail
  is not invisible. It is a slow glide of the directory shells that you can see,
  and on the estate's seed click some of those shells speed up as they slide
  off a shallow balance. Today's rule falls still within a few frames of the
  moment the picture comes within a pixel of its rest. So any rule that "rests
  when what is left to move is invisible" rests later than today's, not sooner.

  Branch commit: `03202789`, on top of `d54755f1` (confirmed with `git log`). It
  holds evidence only: `AI-Agent-Workspace/Screenshots/2026-11-16/motion-rest/`
  with a README, `tail-measurements.json` and four "before" pictures. No source
  change. The scratch probe was taken back out of `forceGraphLayout.ts`. My
  server on port 8957 is stopped.

  ### How it was measured
  - **In the page:** a scratch probe in `motionForce` (never committed),
  headless Chromium at 1440×900, the defaults (Skin, Shell Depth 2, one hop,
  Approximation 0.3), run under the lock. The probe logged:
    - every tick's largest move against the diagonal, and which sphere made it;
    - every frame's screen position of every sphere;
    - the motion's whole state at its last break (a field build, wake, stir or
  hold).
  - **Replayed headless** from that state through the page's own `SpatialField`
  and `SpatialMotion`. The replay matches the page to the last digit: difference
  0 over 1,305, 476, 150 and 111 ticks, and today's rule falls still at the
  same tick.
  - The page reproduced November 15's numbers: 1,309 → 1,309 busy frames, last 1
  px step at frame 186 (same), directory opening 479 → 478 frames.

  ### The tail's log, and what it shows

  | Action | Busy, frames (s here) | Last 1 px step between frames | Last frame
  any sphere is ≥1 px from its rest | Screen travel after that last step |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | Estate seed click | 1,309 (43.0) | 186 | 1,294 | 179 px
  `shell:Portal/Models`, 163 px `shell:Gateway` |
  | Estate, open Portal (Structure) | 478 (13.2) | 131 | 458 | 49 px |
  | Repo, one hop arrival | 257 (62.4) | 192 | 253 | 31 px |
  | Repo whole in Structure | capped at 642 frames / 240 s, 213 ticks | 549 |
  (capped) | still unfolding at the cap |

  November 15's "last visible move" counted a pixel between two consecutive
  frames. A shell gliding a third of a pixel a frame (20 px/s at 60 fps) is
  plainly visible and never counts. With a fixed camera, `shell:Gateway` still
  travels 0.12 of the diagonal (117 px) after frame 186.

  **Estate seed click: neither candidate.**
  - The shells glide in turn, each in a straight line (net displacement over 10
  and 60 ticks equals path length):
    - `shell:Portal/Models` decays (ratio 0.991–0.995 per tick);
    - `shell:Portal/Pages` holds a plateau (~6e-5 of the diagonal per tick);
    - `shell:Gateway` speeds up from 6e-5 to 2.4e-4 per tick (ratio 1.006, ticks
  700–1,000), then decays.
  - Today's rule fires at tick 1,305, in a lull between glides. Run on, the
  shells escape again: Pages from tick 1,350 at ratio 1.002, Models near tick
  4,800 at 1.0045. A true rest comes only after about 7,000 ticks, with Models a
  further 0.18 of the diagonal (87 px) away.
  - These are heavy, soft shells sliding off shallow, unstable balances. Nothing
  in the local motion tells a lull from rest.
  - **At Approximation 0, the same kind of tail.** Today's rule rests at tick
  747, in an earlier lull, and later escapes still follow. The approximation is
  not the cause.

  **The other actions:**
  - **Estate, open Portal:** a clean decay of one hub (`directory:Portal/Pages`)
  at 0.993–0.994 per tick. This is candidate 1. Today's rest is 2.7 px short of
  where it would end.
  - **Repo, one hop:** 168 ticks of unfolding, then a held decay of shells at
  0.98–0.99 per tick. Rest comes 150 ticks after the hold, 2.3 px short.
  - **Repo whole in Structure:** no long tail. It unfolds for 274 ticks, then
  decays at 0.92 per tick and would rest 82 ticks after the hold. At 1,266
  spheres a tick spans about 3 frames here, so the cost is the unfolding and the
  price of a tick, not the rule.
  - **Headless twin (no wrappers, no shells):** short tails. The estate decays
  at 0.9905 (rest at tick 376, 3.3 px short). The repo's pictures show an
  approximation limit cycle of about 1e-6 of the diagonal per tick with
  near-zero net displacement. It vanishes at Approximation 0 and is 30× under
  today's threshold, so it holds nothing up.

  ### Candidate rules on the exact replays
  Ticks to rest, with how far the resting place moves from today's at 1440×900.

  | Rule | Estate seed click | Estate Portal | Repo one hop |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Today (≤3e-5 of the diagonal for 10 ticks) | 1,305 | 476 | 150 |
  | Remaining travel by the 10-tick ratio < 1e-4 | 7,948 (87 px) | 1,363 (2.7
  px) | 389 (2.2 px) |
  | No sphere strays 1e-4 for 30 ticks | 7,136 (87 px) | 891 (2.4 px) | 348 (2.1
  px) |
  | No sphere strays 1e-3 for 30 ticks | 1,303 (0.1 px) | 466 (0.2 px) | 178
  (0.6 px) |
  | Today's rule at 1e-4 | 470 (88 px) | 303 (5.6 px) | 86 (2.7 px) |
  | Today's rule at 3e-4 | 280 (104 px) | 182 (16 px) | 40 (8.4 px) |

  - Every rule that rests when the remainder is under a pixel rests 1.4 to 6
  times later, at the true balance.
  - Every rule that rests sooner freezes a picture that is still visibly
  gliding, somewhere visibly different. That breaks "where a picture comes to
  rest must not change".
  - Today's threshold is already about a speed you cannot see: roughly 0.03 px
  per tick at 1440×900, under 2 px/s at 60 fps.

  ### Decision for you (my recommendation: A)
  - **A. Keep today's rule.** Restate it as what it is, a speed under what the
  eye sees, and add only "held at rest": the motion does not move after
  `onStill`, and velocities are zeroed at rest. Busy time stays long because the
  shells glide slowly. That cause is in the forces (how a heavy, soft shell is
  held), which are outside my brief. A separate round on the forces would
  shorten the glide itself, and it would change where pictures rest.
  - **B. Loosen today's rule.** Shorter busy time, but visibly different resting
  places: 88 px on the seed click.
  - **C. An honest sub-pixel-remainder rule.** Rests at the true balance, but
  busy runs longer, and the two clock-bound cases get redder.

  The sentence "the picture last visibly moved by frame 186" in the Explorer
  design's November 15 building entry (`live-documentation-explorer.mdmd.md`,
  about line 234) is contradicted by this measurement and needs a dated
  correction.

  ### Unit case with a clock
  "walks the tree … opens every cell" takes 0.8 s alone on a quiet machine. It
  does real O(n²) work: 2,000 spheres, summed both exactly and with every cell
  opened, at two repulsion ranges. It only overruns its 5 s clock when the
  machine is loaded. I see nothing wrong in the case itself.

  ### Not done, by design of the stop
  - The rule, the unit tests, the browser case, the decisions log, the Explorer
  design and the Live Docs.
  - The before-and-after busy table: only "before" exists, and it is above.
  - The two clock-bound cases were not run, because nothing changed. No unit
  expectations were moved.
  - One "before" picture is not at rest: `repository-whole-structure-rest.png`
  was taken at the 240 s cap, mid-unfold.
  - The Explorer cannot show motion over time, so there is no `/?` link. The
  evidence folder stands in for it.

  ### Files
  Scratch for a continuation is in `AI-Agent-Workspace/tmp/motion-rest/`, which
  git ignores. I will delete it at the round's end.
  - `measure-page.ts`, `replay.ts`, `rules.ts`, `analyze*.ts`: the measurement
  scripts.
  - `forceGraphLayout.probe.ts`: the probed copy of the motion file.
  - Two large raw logs, including a 60 MB `page-before-CD.json`.

  I used no subagent.

  ## The page and the lab's command report one price again; tip is 7fa83469

  `local-map-simplicity` now passes all 6 cases, including the four agreement
  cases that failed in your gate. I merged main first and found no other
  consumer that compares the two sides. The tree is clean, nothing is pushed or
  amended, and the scratch files are removed.

  Commits on this branch since your message:
  - 86a10118: merge of main at 7eaf6b43 (this includes 695ee0f4).
  - 7fa83469: one price for the order step, the search, the page's report and
  the lab.

  ### Merge conflict
  Only one, as you found: `.mdmd/layer-4/.../branch-renderer.ts.mdmd.md`. I kept
  both authored paragraphs (this round's cycle-trial paragraph first, then
  main's directory-corrections one, both dated 2026-11-16). For "Generated At" I
  took main's line; the generator rewrites it anyway.

  ### Item 1: what fed the two prices, and what I changed
  **Cause.** The round added the wires' horizontal length and the Return Cost of
  each returning link only to the search's prices. The order step's starts
  (`layoutStarts`, so `data-order-score` at the first draw) and the lab's
  `refine` still priced by `scoreOf` alone.
  - That is why `data-search-shown` (105,682) did not match `refine`'s
  `state.shown` (61,397).
  - `data-order-score` was also inconsistent with itself: no terms at the first
  draw, terms after an adoption.

  **Fix: one shared function.** `pictureScore(signals, costs, returnCost, scene,
  branches)` in `branch-restarts.ts` is `scoreOf` plus `rankingTerms`. It is
  now used by:
  - `layoutStarts`, which gains a `returnCost` parameter.
  - The page: the search's first price, a remeasured picture's price, and every
  trial's price (`searchPrice`).
  - The lab: `evaluate`'s starts, `evaluateAlignment`, the cycle walk (whose
  first price no longer adds the terms twice), and `refine`, both its first
  price and every trial.

  **Why `refine` carries the terms even where no cycle offers another order:**
  they are part of the picture's price. These four subjects have no cycle, but
  the horizontal wire is still positive there.

  **Within one ranking the two terms are the same for every start**, so the
  order step still makes the same choice. The full set of specs below confirms
  the pictures are unchanged.

  **Consumers, and what each reads now:**
  - **Read the reported prices and now see one price:**
    - `tests/e2e/local-map-simplicity.spec.ts`: `data-search-shown` against
  `refine`'s `state.shown`. This is the failing case, now equal.
    - `tests/e2e/local-map-search.spec.ts`: `data-search-shown`,
  `data-order-score`, `data-search-priced`, compared with each other.
    - `tests/e2e/layout-lab.spec.ts`: `data-order-score`, chosen start against
  the ranked one, page values only.
    - `scripts/layout-lab/lab.ts` and `refine.ts`: print `state.shown`.
  - **Read `data-placement-cost`, which is the vertical measure (a signal, not a
  price), the same on both sides:** `scripts/layout-lab/capture.ts`,
  `evaluate.driftOf`, `verify.ts`, and `layout-lab.spec`.
  - **Left on `scoreOf` deliberately, and documented:**
    - The renderer's choice of flow shape for opened directories, reported as
  `data-flow-shapes` and read only by `local-map-directories.spec`. Adding the
  terms there would change which shape the page draws, which is a separate
  decision.
    - `scripts/layout-lab/restarts.ts`, a study of start costs within one
  ranking. It reads no page price.

  **Tests:**
  - `branch-restarts.test`: the fixture now carries what the price reads. A new
  case checks `pictureScore` = 300 + 100 + 1500, and that `layoutStarts` prices
  a start the same way.
  - `evaluate.test`: start scores now include the ranking's terms. A new
  assertion checks that `refine`'s `state.shown` is the page's price; it failed
  on the old `refine` (0 against 100).
  - Docs updated: the decisions-log entry, and the Live Docs for
  `branch-restarts.ts` and `refine.ts`.

  ## Motion-rest round, second handback: item 2 needed no code; the rest is done

  **Item 2: a picture said to be at rest already stays put in the page. I built
  nothing.**
  - I watched 600 animation frames after `aria-busy` turned false, with nothing
  touched, in the probed bundle (the probe only logs).
    - Estate seed click: rested in 1,306 ticks (39.5 s). Afterwards the motion
  took 0 ticks and the largest change of any sphere was 0, both in the world and
  on screen. The page drew 0 frames, and `aria-busy` never turned true again.
    - Opening Portal in Structure: rested in 477 ticks (14.2 s). The same zeros.
  - What stops the motion, in `forceGraphView.ts`:
    - `animateFocus` ticks the engine only `if (simulationRunning && motion)`
  (around line 647).
    - `onStill` sets `simulationRunning = false` once the reveal is whole
  (around line 1608).
    - D3's simulation is created stopped, `forceSimulation(...).stop()` at line
  230, so no timer of its own ticks it.
    - Only a wake, a stir, a new piece or a hand starts it again.
  - One thing to know, which nothing triggers today: `motionForce` takes its
  step before it checks `if (still) return`. If anything ever ticked it after
  `onStill`, the spheres would move. Neither the page nor the headless twin,
  whose loop ends at stillness, does that. I wrote this down in the evidence
  README and changed no code.

  **The forces section, in
  `AI-Agent-Workspace/Screenshots/2026-11-16/motion-rest/README.md`:**
  - **Which bodies glide:** only directory bodies drawn whole (shells in
  Connections, bubbles in Structure); files move only as they are carried.
    - Seed click: `shell:Portal/Models` decays from 6e-4 to 3e-5 of the diagonal
  a tick (36 to 2 px/s). `shell:Portal/Pages` holds about 6e-5. `shell:Gateway`
  speeds up from 6e-5 to 2.4e-4 (15 px/s).
    - Portal: `directory:Portal/Pages` decays at 0.993 a tick.
    - Repository at one hop: three shells decay at 0.98–0.99 a tick.
    - Repository whole in Structure: no glide. Once held it decays at 0.92 and
  rests 82 ticks later; the time goes into the 274 ticks of unfolding and each
  tick's price.
  - **What holds a body:**
    - The tether is 0.02. Gravity is 0.025, and Group Separation takes a fifth
  of each group's common pull, leaving 0.02.
    - A body wired to nothing (Models and Pages on the seed click) is a group of
  one, so the part of gravity that holds a group together does nothing for it.
  It is held by the tether plus the common pull, about 0.03 per unit of mass at
  masses 1.26 and 1.44.
    - Mass only slows a body, and the drag of 0.2 takes the same share of every
  body's speed.
    - Softness does nothing during the glides: they touch nothing.
  - **Why the balance is shallow, or no balance:**
    - Around a central mass, gravity and the mass's repulsion cancel sideways,
  which leaves only the tether.
    - Measured curvature along each glide's own line (all spheres moving
  together): about 2e-3 per unit² where Models decays (ticks 300 and 1,300),
  5e-3 at Pages' plateau (tick 650). It goes **negative** where a shell speeds
  up: −1.8e-3 at tick 900 (Gateway), −1.2e-4 at tick 2,000 (Pages, after today's
  rest). The tether alone would give 0.02.
    - The glides are limited by the drag: slope ÷ (mass × drag) reproduces the
  measured speed at tick 300.
  - **Experiments on the exact replay.** Each lever was applied only from the
  last pixel step between frames (ticks 183, 129 and 24), so the arrangement
  before the tail is today's.

  | Lever | Portal opening | Repository at one hop | Seed click |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Weigh every body as one | rests at 427 instead of 476, 0.5 px from today's
  rest | 120 instead of 150, 1.3 px | 46 px from today's rest |
  | Halve the drag | 348, 1.7 px; true rest 680 instead of 1,043 | 132, 2.1 px;
  true rest 305 instead of 470 | 48 px |
  | Double the tether | 184 px from today's rest | 11 px | rests soonest (true
  rest 1,308 instead of 7,371) but 96 px elsewhere |
  | Group Separation 0 | 7 px | 36 px | rests sooner but 95 px elsewhere |
  | Shells as hard as files | unchanged | 1.6 px | unchanged |

    - **On decaying tails** (Portal, repository), the speed levers rest 10–27%
  sooner, and reach a true rest 15–48% sooner, all within about 2 px of today's
  rest.
    - **On the seed click's cascade**, no lever stays within a few pixels. Which
  way a shell slides off decides where the picture ends, and any change of pace
  takes another way.
    - **Costs:** halving the drag acts on every sphere, not only the shells.
  Weighing shells as one undoes the November 7 choice that kept a large shell's
  contacts from shortening every tick's step, so its price in substeps would
  need measuring again.
    - **Suggested first experiments for a forces round:**
      1. The speed levers on shells alone, on more pictures, with their substep
  price.
      2. On the seed click, a steeper sideways hold for the shells.
      3. Whether a rest decided by a lull is acceptable as it is.

    about 1,300 frames (22 s at 60 frames a second). The earlier measurement I
    leaned on only counted jumps of a whole pixel between two frames. Measured
    properly, the directory shells keep gliding more slowly than that, one after
    another. One travels 117 px after the frame I had called the last visible
    move. The picture comes within a pixel of its resting place at frame 1,294
    of 1,309, so the busy sign ends at the right moment.
  - Why the shells crawl. A shell with no wires is held by two weak pulls,
    toward the centre and toward where it stood. Beside the central mass those
    nearly cancel sideways, so the shell sits on an almost flat slope.
    Physicists call this overdamped motion: a spoon sinking through honey never
    overshoots, it just takes long to arrive. You can watch it on the estate's
    groups (http://127.0.0.1:8999/samples/estate/?s=N4IgbiBcDMA0IEcomgExAXyA):
    click a centre sphere and follow Models · 2, Pages · 3 and Gateway · 2.
  - What changed in the code: nothing. The builder tried six other rules for "at
    rest" on exact replays and none was better. Those that wait for the last
    pixel rest 1.4 to 6 times later; looser ones freeze a picture that is still
    gliding. It also checked that once the map says it is at rest, nothing moves
    (600 frames watched, zero movement). The documents that repeated my wrong
    reading now carry dated corrections.
  - What a later round on the forces could buy. Where the tail is one body
    slowing down (opening a directory, arriving on a file here), lighter shells
    or half the drag settle 10 to 27% sooner and end within 2 px of today's
    place. On the seed click, where shells slide off one after another, every
    lever tried ends the picture 46 to 96 px somewhere else. I will bring that

  harness must have received my text and decided to replace it with a summary!!
  I.e., the API response had [thinking][text][tool_use], and the harness — in
  some mode — replaced the text block with a summarized version stored as a
  thinking-typed block (and displayed as "· summary").

  Why would a harness do that? A feature: "condense verbose mid-turn narration
  into a summary" — e.g., when the user is away (not focused) or when the text
  precedes a tool call ("progress narration") and is long, the UI summarizes it
  to keep the transcript compact?! But 14:10:26's text(3752)+tool_use was kept
  verbatim. Hmm, that one was long too.

  Unless the difference is whether the user was viewing/active... The owner sent
  a message at 14:12:18; they were around at 14:10 also.

  OK here's another thought — the system prompt says: "Text you output outside
  of tool use is displayed to the user as Github-flavored markdown in a
  terminal." and the Focus view part. And the harness hint: "The user hasn't
  heard from you in a while — say in a few words what you're doing, then

  12). Then record: ... Now the reply. Keep it short...") ≈ 250–350 tokens.
  Total ≈ 900 ✓. So output tokens include the reply text (as either text or
  thinking). No info.

  Since API-level thinking is billed/treated...

  Decisive test possible? If the reply had been a second thinking block at the
  API level, then in subsequent API calls the harness sends back [thinking(sig),
  thinking(sig), tool_use] and I'd see in my context two thinking blocks. If it
  had been text that the harness replaced by a summary in its transcript, the
  harness would send back [thinking, <summary as thinking?> or the original
  text?]. In my context I see the full original reply text (not a 259-char
  summary). So the full original text is preserved in the conversation state
  sent to the API — consistent with it being a real thinking block at the API
  level (thinking blocks are sent back verbatim-encrypted (signature) and the
  model sees the full original thinking, not the summary!). Yes!! That's it:
  thinking blocks carry the full content for the model (via the
  signature/encrypted payload) while the visible thinking field holds only the
  summary. So in my context, the second thinking block appears with its full
  original content — looking to me just like "text I wrote". And the first
  thinking block likewise appears with full content (though logged with empty
  summary — because... the summarizer was given both blocks and attributes its
  summary to the last one? Or the first block's summary is omitted since summary
  rows are produced once per response, attached to the final thinking block).

  So: at the API level, in failing responses my reply was emitted in a second
  thinking block. I.e., after finishing the first reasoning block, I opened
  another thinking block and wrote the reply there, then called the tool. In my
  context, how are two consecutive thinking blocks rendered? Perhaps as
  [thinking]A[/thinking] then B after the "" marker?... The marker "" may be
  what separates blocks in my view. I can't tell the difference between a text
  block and a 2nd thinking block in my own context rendering. Hmm, that's quite
  a handicap. But it explains everything, including "with both 0" (when the
  reply goes to a 2nd thinking block there's no text block).

  Why would I emit a second thinking block instead of text? Because of
  adaptive/interleaved thinking: after one thinking block, the model can
  continue with another. The model "decides" by emitting a special token. It's
  stochastic and may be influenced by the nature of the content: a long
  deliberate "composed reply" may be sampled as thinking...

  How can I bias toward text? Perhaps by making the reply start in an
  unmistakably user-facing way and keeping my final reasoning explicitly
  concluded. I can't control special tokens consciously... but maybe I can: the
  failure may correlate with me ending the reasoning with an instruction to
  myself like "Write it." Not known.

  The robust approach remains: verify, and for critical messages use a response
  with no tool call (text only), in which case the API must emit text (a
  response with only thinking and no text/tool_use would be an empty response;
  the model would produce text). Evidence: every toolless response has text. But
  a toolless response ends my turn. Is that acceptable? The background job will
  wake me. And "ScheduleWakeup"? Not needed: the Playwright job b1sdr8t2d will
  notify me upon exit, and the legend builder will hand back.

  But wait: would a toolless response with the reply in a second thinking block
  and then a short text be possible ("thinking, thinking(reply), text('')")? The
  model must emit some text to end; if my reply went into thinking again, the
  final text would be something else or empty. Risk exists but lower.

  Alternatively: deliver the reply via a tool that definitely shows content to
  the user: e.g., AskUserQuestion? No. Artifact? no. PushNotification (deferred
  tool)? That pushes a notification to the user — might be visible; but content
  reliability... Hmm. Not appropriate for long content.

  unknown whether I tried).

  It might be that the sampling of "text vs thinking" at the block boundary is
  influenced by the first tokens... no wait, the block type is decided before
  the first content token (the model emits a block-start control token first,
  then content). So the content's first words can't influence the block type
  causally — but they correlate with my "mode": when I'm in "formal
  announcement" mode (bold lead), I emit text; when in "conversational
  continuation" mode (plain prose continuing the flow of thought), I emit
  another thinking block. The decision is made by me-the-model at the boundary:
  "am I still thinking, or speaking?" Conversational replies that flow straight
  from deliberation ("You're right, ...") are sampled as thinking.

  So a practical trick: begin every owner-facing message with a formal, bold
  lead sentence, as a deliberate "speaking now" act; and verify.

● Backward connections is committed (01b4cd2f) and on your port 8999. On main:
  69 of 69 browser cases, including the four the first gate found red.

  What it does

  Where files use each other in a ring, some connection has to run right to left
  whatever the order. Graph-drawing people call those the feedback arcs; on our
  screen they are the amber feet on the cards and the count on the 3D button.

  - The first picture is drawn exactly as before.
  - Then the background search tries the ring's other orders, the same search
    that already tries other arrangements once a picture is up. It moves to one
    only if the whole picture comes out cheaper.
  - A backward connection is one cost among the others (width, height, wire
    length, crossings). That is your "competing concerns, emergently", and it
    has a lever: Tuning → 2D Layout → Return Cost.

  The one thing you will notice

  A picture with a ring may rearrange once, a second or more after it arrives,
  and the 3D button's count follows. On the builder's run that was about 1 s on
  go.ts and manifest.ts, and about 5 s on branch-renderer.ts.

  See it

  Open each and wait for the rearrangement:

  - manifest.ts (http://127.0.0.1:8999/?view=local&node=packages/engine/src/live
    -docs/adapters/manifest.ts): 1 backward connection becomes 0, six columns
    become four, and the 3D button's amber goes out. The clearest win.
  - go.ts (http://127.0.0.1:8999/?view=local&node=packages/engine/src/live-docs/
    adapters/go.ts): 3 become 1, and the picture stays readable when you press
    Fit.
  - branch-renderer.ts (http://127.0.0.1:8999/?view=local&node=packages/explorer
    /src/client/views/localView/branch-renderer.ts): 8 become 7. Slight.
  - adapters/index.ts (http://127.0.0.1:8999/?view=local&node=packages/engine/sr
    c/live-docs/adapters/index.ts): 34 become 22, but in one tall column. Mixed:
    it scores better and is no easier to read at Fit.
  - branch-scene.ts (http://127.0.0.1:8999/?view=local&node=packages/explorer/sr
    c/client/views/localView/branch-scene.ts): 4 become 6. More run backward
    here because the picture gets narrower with shorter wires, which shows the
    cost is a weight and not a ban.

  Across the 33 pictures that change: backward connections 258 to 197, wire down
  a tenth, crossings down a quarter.

  this file". It now ends "· Open this group and pin this file", which is what
  a press does. `force-graph-wrappers.spec.ts` asserts it.

  large dot, thin and thick line) to say size and width.
  - **"Cites" and "Cited by"** are two near-identical purple arrows told apart
  only by the word. That is a colour question, not this round's.
  - **"Directory shell"** keeps the product's own word "shell", which the map's
  tooltip also uses.

  ### Forks decided
  - **Capitalisation**: the two role words stay capitalised, "Offers" and
  "Uses", as the other rows are.
  - **Kinds row committed present**: the 2D kinds row is in the template, so its
  tests can run. If the owner prefers the hover alone, delete:
    - the template's one line;
    - `watchLocalKinds` and its call;
    - `#local-legend-kinds`'s two rules in `local.css`;
    - the kinds assertions in the two browser cases.
  - **How the kinds row knows what is drawn**: it reads the kinds from the
  cells' own `fill`, through a `MutationObserver` on `#map-container`, so the 2D
  map's files are untouched. The alternative was a call from the 2D controller,
  which is map code.
  - **Kinds row alignment**: its dots sit tight to their words (6 px), with the
  first dot centred in the sample column, so its words stand 9 px left of the
  other rows' word column. I chose grouping over that alignment; your eye may
  differ.

  ### Unfinished or still wrong
  - **No keyboard path to a row's sentence.** The sentences are native `title`
  tooltips; a sighted keyboard-only reader cannot open one, since rows are not
  focusable. Tuning's labels have the same limit.
  - **Test-backed is always shown**, including on the estate sample, which has
  no tests. That breaks "only what is on screen" and predates this round. One
  CSS `:has()` line would fix it; I left it under "nothing else moves".
  - **At the overview look of a small picture** the kinds row is shown for cells
  drawn only a few pixels across.
  - **Tooltips were not seen as tooltips**: headless only.

  - Both servers are stopped (8956 was pid 564326; the temporary base-bundle
  server on 8966 was killed by its script).
  - Scratch scripts, site copies and raw shots are deleted. Run logs are kept in
  the worktree's `AI-Agent-Workspace/tmp/legend-words/` (git-ignored).
  - `test-results/` and `reports/e2e` are removed. The worktree is clean.
